.

Proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Due to Lemma
3.11

(iii)
and Fact
3.1
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})+e(\mathcal{A}_{6},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})\leq O(n)+\begin{cases}\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6},&\quad\text{if}\quad a_{6}\leq b+c,\\[5.0pt]
8a_{6}^{2}+\left(\frac{13b}{2}+\frac{9c}{2}\right)a_{6},&\quad\text{if}\quad a_{6}\geq b+c.\end{cases}

Let us define an auxiliary graph
H
whose vertex set is
\mathcal{A}_{6}
and two members
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
are adjacent in
H
iff
e(Q_{1},Q_{2})=16
.
Notice from the definition of
H
that

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq 6a_{6}+16|H|+15\left(\binom{a_{6}}{2}-|H|\right)=6a_{6}+15\binom{a_{6}}{2}+|H|.

(22)

It follows from Lemma
3.11
that for every pair
\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\}\in\overline{H}
, we have

\displaystyle e(Q_{1}\cup Q_{2},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})\leq 2\cdot 7\cdot(b-2)+2\cdot 3\cdot 8+2\cdot 5\cdot(c-1)+8=14b+10c+18.

(23)

It follows from Lemma
6.3
that for every pair
\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\}\in H
, we have

\displaystyle e(Q_{1}\cup Q_{2},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})\leq 13(b-1)+3\cdot 8+9(c-1)+2\cdot 8=13b+9c+18.

(24)

Combining (
23
) and (
24
) and using the fact
|H|+|\overline{H}|=\binom{a_{6}}{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a_{6}-1}\sum_{\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{A}_{6}}e(Q_{1}\cup Q_{2},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{a_{6}-1}\left(\sum_{\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\}\in H}e(Q_{1}\cup Q_{2},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})+\sum_{\{Q_{1},Q_{2}\}\in\overline{H}}e(Q_{1}\cup Q_{2},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(13b+9c+18)|H|+(14b+10c+18)|\overline{H}|}{a_{6}-1}=(7b+5c+9)a_{6}-\frac{(b+c)|H|}{a_{6}-1}.

Combining this with (
22
), we obtain

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})+e(\mathcal{A}_{6},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\leq 6a_{6}+15\binom{a_{6}}{2}+|H|+(7b+5c+9)a_{6}-\frac{(b+c)|H|}{a_{6}-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6}+\frac{15a_{6}}{2}+\left(1-\frac{b+c}{a_{6}-1}\right)|H|

\displaystyle=\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6}+\frac{a_{6}-(b+c)}{a_{6}-1}|H|+\frac{15a_{6}}{2}.

(25)

If
a_{6}>b+c
, then
\frac{a_{6}-(b+c)}{a_{6}-1}>0
, and hence, (
25
) continues as

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})+e(\mathcal{A}_{6},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6}+\frac{a_{6}-(b+c)}{a_{6}-1}\binom{a_{6}}{2}+\frac{15a_{6}}{2}

\displaystyle=8a_{6}^{2}+\frac{13b+9c}{2}a_{6}+\frac{15a_{6}}{2},

as desired.

If
a_{6}\leq b+c
, then
\frac{a_{6}-(b+c)}{a_{6}-1}\leq 0
, and hence, (
25
) continues as

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})+e(\mathcal{A}_{6},\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6}+\frac{a_{6}-(b+c)}{a_{6}-1}\cdot 0+\frac{15a_{6}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{6}+\frac{15a_{6}}{2},

also as desired.
This completes the proof of Lemma
6.1
.

6.2
Preparations for Lemma
6.2
I: an extension of Turán

The aim of this subsection is to establish the following result (Proposition
6.4
), which will be crucial for the proof of Lemma
6.2
.

For integers
h,\alpha\geq 0
satisfying
h\geq 3\alpha
, let

\displaystyle P(h,\alpha)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha)+12h,&\quad\text{if}\quad 3\alpha\leq h<16\alpha,\\[5.0pt]
\binom{h-3\alpha+9}{2}+(\alpha-3)(2h-3\alpha+9),&\quad\text{if}\quad h\geq 16\alpha.\end{cases}

(26)

Simple calculations show that

\displaystyle P(h,\alpha)\leq\frac{(h-\alpha)^{2}}{2}+\alpha^{2}+\frac{23h}{2}+\frac{3\alpha}{2}+9.

(27)

Proposition 6.4
.

There exists a constant
N_{\ref{PROP:K4-tiling-cover-three-vtx}}>0
such that the following holds for all
h\geq N_{\ref{PROP:K4-tiling-cover-three-vtx}}
.
Suppose that
H
is a graph on
h
vertices and there exists a vertex set
A\subseteq V(H)
of size
\alpha
such that every
K_{4}
-tiling in
H
contains at most three vertices in
A
. Then

\displaystyle|H|\leq P(h,\alpha).

We will need the following result (which is a special case of
[
ABHP14
, Theorem 2]
) by Allen–Böttcher–Hladký–Piguet
[
ABHP14
]
.

Let
\mathcal{T}_{3}(h,\alpha)
denote the family of all
h
-vertex graphs
H
satisfying the following properties:

•

The vertex set of
H
can be partitioned into seven parts:
X
,
Y_{1},Y_{2},Y_{3}
,
Z_{1},Z_{2},Z_{3}
, such that

\displaystyle\alpha\leq|Y_{1}\cup Z_{1}|\leq|Y_{2}\cup Z_{2}|\leq|Y_{3}\cup Z_{3}|\leq\alpha+1\quad\text{and}\quad|Z_{1}|+|Z_{2}|+|Z_{3}|=\alpha.

•

The edge set of
H
is given by

\displaystyle H=\binom{X}{2}\cup K[X,Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}\cup Y_{3}]\cup K[Y_{1}\cup Z_{1},Y_{2}\cup Z_{2},Y_{3}\cup Z_{3}].

Theorem 6.5

(Allen–Böttcher–Hladký–Piguet
[
ABHP14
]
)
.

Let
h,\alpha\geq 0
be integers satisfying
h\geq 3\alpha
.
Suppose that
H
is a graph on
h
vertices and
A\subseteq V(H)
is a vertex set of size
\alpha
such that not copy of
K_{4}
in
H
has nonempty intersection with
A
.
Then

\displaystyle|H|\leq\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+3\alpha^{2}+(h-3\alpha)\cdot 2\alpha=\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha\cdot(2h-3\alpha).

Furthermore, for every
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
and
N_{0}
such that if
h\geq N_{0}
and

\displaystyle|H|\geq\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha)-\delta h^{2},

then
H
is a member in
\mathcal{T}_{3}(h,\alpha)
after removing and adding at most
\varepsilon h^{2}
edges.

The following simple but useful property of
\mathcal{T}_{3}(h,\alpha)
follows from its definition and elementary calculations (a detailed proof is provided in the Appendix).

Proposition 6.6
.

Let
h,\alpha\geq 0
be integers satisfying
h\geq 16\alpha
. Suppose that
H\in\mathcal{T}_{3}(h,\alpha)
. Then for every vertex set
S\subseteq V(H)
of size at least
h-12\alpha-42
, we have

\displaystyle|H[S]|\geq\binom{s-\alpha}{2}.

We are now ready to present the proof of Proposition
6.4
.

Proof of Proposition
6.4
.

Fix
\varepsilon>0
. We may assume that
\varepsilon
is sufficiently small. Let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small and
h
be sufficiently large.
Let
H
be a graph on
h
vertices that satisfies the assumptions of Proposition
6.4
. Let
V\coloneqq V(H)
.
Let
\mathcal{K}
be a
K_{4}
-tiling in
H
such that
|V(\mathcal{K})\cap A|
is maximized. Additionally, among all such
K_{4}
-tilings, assume that
|\mathcal{K}|
is minimized.
It follows from the minimality of
|\mathcal{K}|
and the assumption
|V(\mathcal{K})\cap A|\leq 3
that
|\mathcal{K}|\leq 3
. Therefore,
|V(\mathcal{K})|\leq 12
. Let
U\subseteq V
be a set of size
12
containing
V(\mathcal{K})
such that
|U\cap A|=3
.

Let
H^{\prime}
be the graph obtained from
H
by removing all edges that have nonempty intersection with
U
, noting that we lose at most
12h
edges. It follows from the maximality of
|V(\mathcal{K})\cap A|
that no copy of
K_{4}
in
H^{\prime}
contains a vertex from
A
.
Hence, by Theorem
6.5
, we have

\displaystyle|H^{\prime}|=|H[V\setminus U]|

\displaystyle\leq\binom{h-12-3(\alpha-3)}{2}+(\alpha-3)\left(2(h-12)-3(\alpha-3)\right)

(28)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha).

Suppose that
h\leq 16\alpha
. Then it follows from the inequality above that

\displaystyle|H|\leq|H^{\prime}|+12h\leq\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha)+12h\leq P(h,\alpha),

as desired.
So we may assume that
h>16\alpha
.

Since
h>16\alpha
and
|H|>P(h,\alpha)-12
(we are done if
|H|\leq P(h,\alpha)-12
), it follows from the definition of
P(h,\alpha)
that

\displaystyle|H|

\displaystyle>\binom{h-3\alpha+6}{2}+(2\alpha-3)(h-3\alpha+6)+3(\alpha-2)^{2}-12h

\displaystyle=\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha)-9(h+\alpha-1)\geq\binom{h-3\alpha}{2}+\alpha(2h-3\alpha)-\delta h^{2}.

By the stability part of Theorem
6.5
, there exists a member
\hat{H}\in\mathcal{T}_{3}(h,\alpha)
such that
\mathrm{edit}(H,\hat{H})\leq\varepsilon h^{2}
. Combining this with Proposition
6.6
, we obtain that for every set
S\subseteq V
of size at least
h-12\alpha-42\geq\max\left\{4\alpha-42,\frac{25\alpha}{11}+\frac{25}{22},~\frac{3h}{16}\right\}
,

\displaystyle|H[S]|

\displaystyle\geq|\hat{H}[S]|-\mathrm{edit}(H,\hat{H})\geq\binom{|S|-\alpha}{2}-\varepsilon h^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{28|S|^{2}}{100}-\varepsilon h^{2}+\frac{11}{50}\left(|S|-\frac{25\alpha}{11}-\frac{25}{22}\right)^{2}-\frac{56\alpha^{2}+56\alpha-25}{88}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{28|S|^{2}}{100}-\varepsilon h^{2}+\frac{11}{50}\left(4\alpha-42-\frac{25\alpha}{11}-\frac{25}{22}\right)^{2}-\frac{56\alpha^{2}+56\alpha-25}{88}

\displaystyle=\frac{28|S|^{2}}{100}-\varepsilon h^{2}+\frac{1}{50}\left(\alpha-\frac{1671}{2}\right)^{2}-\frac{108417}{8}\geq\left(\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{100}\right)|S|^{2},

where the last inequality follows from the assumption that
h
is sufficiently large,
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small, and
|S|\geq h-12\alpha-42\geq\frac{3h}{16}
.

It follows from Theorem
1.2
that

\displaystyle\nu(K_{3},H[S])\geq 13\quad\text{for every subset $S\subseteq V$ of size at least $h-12\alpha-42$.}

(29)

Let
v_{1},v_{2},v_{3}
denote the vertices in
U\cap A
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle d_{H}(v_{1})+d_{H}(v_{2})+d_{H}(v_{3})\leq 3h-12\alpha-42.

Then it follows from (
28
) that

\displaystyle|H|

\displaystyle\leq|H^{\prime}|+|U\setminus A|\cdot h+d_{H}(v_{1})+d_{H}(v_{2})+d_{H}(v_{3})

\displaystyle\leq\binom{h-12-3(\alpha-3)}{2}+(\alpha-3)\left(2(h-12)-3(\alpha-3)\right)+9h+3h-12\alpha-42

\displaystyle=\binom{h-3\alpha+6}{2}+(2\alpha-3)(h-3\alpha+6)+3(\alpha-3)^{2}=P(h,\alpha),

as desired. So we may assume that
d_{H}(v_{1})+d_{H}(v_{2})+d_{H}(v_{3})\geq 3h-12\alpha-42
. In particular,

\displaystyle\min\left\{|N_{H}(v_{i})|\colon i\in[3]\right\}\geq d_{H}(v_{1})+d_{H}(v_{2})+d_{H}(v_{3})-2h\geq h-12\alpha-42.

By (
29
), for each
i\in[3]
, there exists a
K_{3}
-tiling
\mathcal{T}_{i}
of size at least
13
in the induced subgraph
H[N_{H}(v_{i})]
.
Note that every member in
\mathcal{T}_{i}
forms a copy of
K_{4}
with
v_{i}
.

Claim 6.7
.

There is no copy of
K_{4}
in
G
that contains a vertex from
A\setminus U
.

Proof of Claim
6.7
.

Suppose to the contrary that there exists a copy of
K_{4}
in
G
, denoted by
Q
, that contains at least one vertex from
A\setminus U
. Then a simple greedy argument shows that there is a section of
T_{i}\in\mathcal{T}_{i}
for each
i\in[3]
such that
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},Q
are pairwise disjoint.
Let
Q_{i}\coloneqq T_{i}\cup\{v_{i}\}
for
i\in[3]
. Note the
\{Q,Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3}\}
is a
K_{4}
-tiling in
G
that contains at least four vertices in
A
, contradicting the assumption of Proposition
6.4
.

It follows from Claim
6.7
and Theorem
6.5
that

\displaystyle|H|\leq\binom{h-3(\alpha-3)}{2}+(\alpha-3)\cdot(2h-3(\alpha-3))=P(h,\alpha),

as desired. This completes the proof of Proposition
6.4
.

6.3
Preparations for Lemma
6.2
II: connecting

In this subsection, we establish several local properties of
\mathcal{A}_{6}
, which will be useful for the proofs in the next subsection.

The following definition of
connection
is inspired by that in
[
ABHP15
]
.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
Q_{1},Q_{2},Q_{3}\subseteq V(G)
be three pairwise disjoint sets of size
4
, with each inducing a copy of
K_{4}
in
G
. We say that
Q_{2}

connects

Q_{1}
and
Q_{3}
(or that there exists a connection from
Q_{1}
to
Q_{3}
via
Q_{2}
), denoted by
Q_{1}\rightsquigarrow Q_{2}\rightsquigarrow Q_{3}
, if one of the following conditions holds
\colon

(i)

\min\left\{|G[Q_{1},Q_{2}]|,~|G[Q_{2},Q_{3}]|\right\}\geq 15
.

(ii)

|G[Q_{1},Q_{2}]|=16
and
|G[Q_{2},Q_{3}]|\geq 14
.

Q_{1}
Q_{2}
Q_{3}
Q_{1}
Q_{2}
Q_{3}
Q_{1}
Q_{2}
Q_{3}
Q_{1}
Q_{2}
Q_{3}
(a)
(b)
(c)
(d)

Figure 38:
Auxiliary figure for Proposition
6.9
.

The following proposition follows easily from the definition and Fact
2.7
; its proof is omitted here.

Proposition 6.9
.

Suppose that
Q_{1}\rightsquigarrow Q_{2}\rightsquigarrow Q_{3}
. Then the following statements hold.

(i)

For every pair of vertices
\{p_{1},q_{1}\}\subseteq Q_{1}
, there exists a pair of vertices
\{p_{2},q_{2}\}\subseteq Q_{2}
that are adjacent to both
p_{1}
and
q_{1}
, such that the remaining two vertices in
Q_{2}\setminus\{p_{2},q_{2}\}
have at least three common neighbors in
Q_{3}
(see Figure
38
(a)).

(ii)

For every pair of vertices
\{p_{3},q_{3}\}\subseteq Q_{3}
, there exists a pair of vertices
\{p_{2},q_{2}\}\subseteq Q_{2}
that are adjacent to both
p_{3}
and
q_{3}
, such that the remaining two vertices in
Q_{2}\setminus\{p_{2},q_{2}\}
have at least three common neighbors in
Q_{1}
(see Figure
38
(b)).

(iii)

For every vertex
p_{1}\in Q_{1}
, there exists three vertices
\{p_{2},q_{2},s_{2}\}\subseteq Q_{2}
that are all adjacent to
p_{1}
, such that the remaining vertex in
Q_{2}\setminus\{p_{2},q_{2},s_{2}\}
has at least three neighbors in
Q_{3}
(see Figure
38
(c)).

(iv)

For every triple of vertices
\{p_{3},q_{3},s_{3}\}\subseteq Q_{3}
, there exists a vertex
p_{2}\in Q_{2}
that is adjacent to all three vertices in
\{p_{3},q_{3},s_{3}\}
, such that the remaining three vertices in
Q_{2}\setminus\{p_{2}\}
have at least three common neighbors in
Q_{1}
(see Figure
38
(d)).

For convenience, for every pair
Q_{1},Q_{3}
of copies of
K_{4}
in
\mathcal{A}_{6}
, let

\displaystyle N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}(Q_{1},Q_{3})\coloneqq\left\{Q\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\setminus\{Q_{1},Q_{3}\}\colon Q_{1}\rightsquigarrow Q\rightsquigarrow Q_{3}\right\}.

For every
Q\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
, let

\displaystyle N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)\coloneqq\left\{Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\colon e(Q,Q^{\prime})\leq 14\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q)\coloneqq\left\{Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\colon e(Q,Q^{\prime})\geq 15\right\}.

Note that
\mathcal{A}_{6}\setminus\{Q\}=N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)\cup N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q)
is a partition.

Lemma 6.10
.

For every member
Q\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)|\leq\frac{a_{6}-1}{2}.

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

Suppose to the contrary that
|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)|>\frac{a_{6}-1}{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle e(Q,\mathcal{A}_{6}\setminus\{Q\})\leq 14\cdot|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)|+16\left(|\mathcal{A}_{6}|-1-|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)|\right)

\displaystyle=16(|\mathcal{A}_{6}|-1)-2|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q)|

\displaystyle<15(|\mathcal{A}_{6}|-1),

contradicting the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{6}
(see Section
1.3
).

Lemma 6.11
.

For every pair
Q_{1},Q_{3}
of members in
\mathcal{A}_{6}
, we have

\displaystyle|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}(Q_{1},Q_{3})|\geq\frac{a_{6}-2}{12}.

In particular, for every subset
B\subseteq V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
of size less than
\frac{a_{6}-2}{12}
, there exists a member
Q\in\mathcal{A}_{6}-B
such that
Q_{1}\rightsquigarrow Q\rightsquigarrow Q_{3}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.11
.

Fix two distinct members
Q_{1},Q_{3}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
. Observe that, by definition, we have

\displaystyle N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}(Q_{1},Q_{3})\subseteq N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{1})\cap N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{3}).

If
|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{1})|\geq\frac{7(a_{6}-2)}{12}
, then it follows from Lemma
6.10
that

\displaystyle|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}(Q_{1},Q_{3})|\geq|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{1})|-|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{3})|\geq\frac{a_{6}-1}{12},

as desired.
So we may assume that
|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{1})|<\frac{7(a_{6}-2)}{12}
. This implies that

\displaystyle|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{1})|=(a_{6}-1)-|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{\ell}(Q_{1})|>\frac{5(a_{6}-1)}{12}.

(30)

By symmetry, we may also assume that

\displaystyle|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{3})|>\frac{5(a_{6}-1)}{12}.

(31)

Let
x\coloneqq|\left\{Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\colon e(Q_{1},Q^{\prime})=16\right\}|
.
It follows from the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{6}
that

\displaystyle 15(a_{6}-1)<e(Q_{1},\mathcal{A}_{6}\setminus\{Q_{1}\})

\displaystyle\leq 16x+15\left((a_{6}-1)-x-|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{1})|\right)+14\cdot|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{1})|

\displaystyle=x+15(a_{6}-1)-|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{1})|,

which, by (
30
), implies that

\displaystyle x>|N_{\mathcal{A}_{6}}^{s}(Q_{1})|>\frac{5(a_{6}-1)}{12}.

Let
y\coloneqq|\left\{Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\colon e(Q_{3},Q^{\prime})\geq 14\right\}|
.
Similarly, it follows from the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{6}
that

\displaystyle 15(a_{6}-1)<e(Q_{3},\mathcal{A}_{6}\setminus\{Q_{3}\})<16y+13\left((a_{6}-1)-y\right)=15(a_{6}-1)+3y-2(a_{6}-1),

which implies that

\displaystyle y>\frac{2(a_{6}-1)}{3}.

Therefore, by the Inclusion-Exclusion Principle, the set

\displaystyle\left\{Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}\colon e(Q_{1},Q^{\prime})=16~\text{and}~e(Q_{3},Q^{\prime})\geq 14\right\},

which contains
N_{\mathcal{A}_{3}}(Q_{1},Q_{3})
, has size at least

\displaystyle x+y-(a_{6}-1)>\frac{5(a_{6}-1)}{12}+\frac{2(a_{6}-1)}{3}-(a_{6}-1)=\frac{a_{6}-1}{12},

as desired.
This completes the proof of Lemma
6.11
.

Given a vertex
Z\subseteq V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
, we denote by
\mathcal{A}_{6}-Z
the collection of members in
\mathcal{A}_{6}
that have an empty intersection with
Z
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\mu\geq 0
be an integer.
Suppose that

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}.

Then for every subset
Z\subseteq V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
of size at most
\mu-3
, there exist six distinct members

\displaystyle Q_{1},Q_{1}^{\prime},Q_{2},Q_{2}^{\prime},Q_{3},Q_{3}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}-Z

such that, for each
i\in[3]
,
Q_{i}\cup Q_{i}^{\prime}
induces a copy of
K_{8}
in
G
.

Proof of Lemma
6.12
.

Let us define an auxiliary graph
H
whose vertex set is
\mathcal{A}_{6}
and two elements
Q,Q^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
are adjacent in
H
iff
e(Q,Q^{\prime})=16
.
Observe that it suffices to show that
\nu(H)\geq\mu\geq|Z|+3
.
By Theorem
2.1
, this reduces to showing that

\displaystyle|H|>\mu a_{6}.

Suppose to the contrary that
|H|\leq\mu a_{6}
. Then

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq 16\cdot|H|+15\cdot\left(\binom{a_{6}}{2}-|H|\right)+6a_{6}<\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+|H|\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6},

contradicting the assumption that
|H|\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
.

6.4
Preparations for Lemma
6.2
III: six operations

In the proof of Lemma
6.2
, we will employ six operations (rotations).
In this subsection, we define these six operations, establish their existence in
\mathcal{A}_{6}
, and state the consequences of each operation.

Throughout this subsection, we assume the following condition holds:

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6},

(32)

where
\mu>0
is a fixed (large) integer.
Since
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq\binom{4a_{6}}{2}\leq 8a_{6}^{2}
holds trivially, it follows from (
32
) that
a_{6}\geq 2\mu
.

The following lemma establishes the existence of Operations I and II (as shown in Figures
39
and
40
).

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
Q_{1},Q_{3}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}
be two distinct members, with
\{p_{1},q_{1}\}\subseteq Q_{1}
as two fixed vertices in
Q_{1}
and
p_{3}\in Q_{3}
as a fixed vertex in
Q_{3}
.
For every subset
B\subseteq V(\mathcal{A}_{6})\setminus(Q_{1}\cup Q_{3})
of size less than
\frac{a_{6}-2}{12}
, there exists a member
Q_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}-(Q_{1}\cup Q_{3}\cup B)
such that
\colon

(i)

there exists a vertex
p_{2}\in Q_{2}
that is adjacent to all three vertices in
Q_{3}\setminus\{p_{3}\}
, and

(ii)

the remaining three vertices in
Q_{2}\setminus\{p_{2}\}
have a common neighbor in
Q_{1}\setminus\{p_{1},q_{1}\}
.

Remark.
We call
Q_{1}Q_{2}Q_{3}
a
switching
path (
avoiding

B
and
connecting

Q_{1}
and
Q_{3}
) and refer to
Q_{1}
and
Q_{3}
as the
ends
of this switching path.

Proof of Lemma
6.13
.

This lemma is a direct consequence of Lemma
6.11
and Propositions
6.9

(iv)
\colon

Given that
\frac{a_{6}-2}{12}>|B|
, Lemma
6.11
guarantees the existence of a member
Q_{2}\in\mathcal{A}_{6}-B
such that
Q_{1}\rightsquigarrow Q_{2}\rightsquigarrow Q_{3}
. Consequently, the assertions in Lemma
6.13
follows from Propositions
6.9

(iv)
.

6.5
Proof of Lemma
6.2

In this subsection, we present the proof of Lemma
6.2
.
Note that by Proposition
6.4
and (
27
), it suffices to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 6.18
.

Let
A\coloneqq V(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{D})
,
U\coloneqq V(\mathcal{A}_{6})\cup A
, and
H\coloneqq G[U]
.
There exists an absolute constant
\mu>0
such that the following holds.
Suppose that

\displaystyle e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}.

Then every
K_{4}
-tiling in
H
contains at most three vertices in
A
.

Remark.
Applying Proposition
6.4
to
H
with
h=4a_{6}+3b+2c+d
and
\alpha=3b+2c+d
, we obtain Lemma
6.2
.

Proof of Lemma
6.18
.

Let
\mu>0
be a sufficiently large integer. Recall from Section
6.4
(see the argument after (
32
)) that we have
a_{6}\geq 2\mu
is also sufficiently large.
Suppose to the contrary that there exists a
K_{4}
-tiling
\mathcal{S}
in
H
such that
|V(\mathcal{S})\cap A|\geq 4
. Among all such
K_{4}
-tilings, select one with the smallest size.
Recall from the maximality of
(|\mathcal{A}|,|\mathcal{B}|,|\mathcal{C}|,|\mathcal{D}|)
that
H[A]
is
K_{4}
-free.

Observe that every member in
\mathcal{S}
must contain at least one vertex from
V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
, and therefore must be of Type I, II, or III, as defined in Figure
45
below.

V(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{D})
V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
Type I
Type II
Type III

Figure 45:
Three types of
K_{4}
crossing
V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
and
V(\mathcal{B}\cup\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{D})
. Red vertices are currently covered by two

’s (crossing
K_{4}
’s and
K_{4}
’s within
\mathcal{A}_{6}
).

By the minimality of

, the number of different types of
K_{4}
in

falls into one of the following six cases
\colon

.

.

.

(iv)

2\times\text{Type {II}}
.

(v)

.

(vi)

2\times\text{Type {III}}
.

A
V(\mathcal{A}_{6})

Figure 46:
Left: the set of marked vertices (red) are of type
4+4+4
. Right: after rotation, the number of vertex-disjoint copies of

increases by one.

Let
Z\coloneqq V(\mathcal{S})\cap V(\mathcal{A}_{6})
.
Let
\mathcal{Z}
be the collection of members in
\mathcal{A}_{6}
that have nonempty intersection with

.
Observe that we must have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Z}|\geq|\mathcal{S}|,

(33)

since otherwise, we could increased the size of
\mathcal{A}
by updating it to
(\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{Z})\cup\mathcal{S}
(see e.g. Figure

), contradicting the maximality of
(|\mathcal{A}|,|\mathcal{B}|,|\mathcal{C}|,|\mathcal{D}|)
(in particular, the maximality of
|\mathcal{A}|
).

Let

\displaystyle Z_{0}\coloneqq Z,\quad\mathcal{Z}_{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{Z},\quad\mathcal{S}_{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{S},\quad B_{0}\coloneqq V(\mathcal{Z}_{0}\cup\mathcal{S}_{0}),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{K}_{0}\coloneqq\mathcal{A}_{6}\cup\mathcal{S}.

Suppose that we have defined
Z_{i}
,
\mathcal{Z}_{i}
,
\mathcal{S}_{i}
,

,

for some

.
For a sequence of descending positive integers
(\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{m})\in[4]^{m}
that satisfies
\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{m}=12
, we say that
Z_{i}
is of

\alpha_{1}\ldots\alpha_{m}
if there exist
m
distinct members
Q_{1},\ldots,Q_{m}\in\mathcal{K}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{i}
such that

|Q_{j}\cap Z_{i}|=\alpha_{j}
for each
j\in[m]
.

The length
m
of the tuple
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{m})
is denoted by
\mathrm{length}(Z_{i})
.
For instance, the set
Z
(of red vertices) in Figure

is of type
444
and
\mathrm{length}(Z)=3
.

Define

,

,

,

using the following process:

(i)

Note that for every possible type of
Z_{i}
, there is a corresponding operation (with some cases having more than one operation) listed in Figure
47
. For example, Operation 1 and Operation 2 are the corresponding operations for type
21111111111
.

Choose an operation according to the type of
Z_{i}
and Figure
47
(if there are at least two options, select one arbitrarily). Denote the chosen operation by
\mathrm{Ope}_{Z_{i}}
.

(ii)

Given the operation
\mathrm{Ope}_{Z_{i}}
, we need to construct a switching path. We begin by fixing the ends (which consist of at most four distinct members
Q_{j}\in\mathcal{Z}_{i}
) for this switching path, which are chosen according
\mathrm{Ope}_{Z_{i}}
.
For example, if
\mathrm{Ope}_{Z_{i}}
is Operation 2 (recall that the function of Operation 2 is
2+1\to 3
), then we fix ends
Q_{1},Q_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}_{i}
such that
|Q_{1}\cap Z_{i}|=2
and
|Q_{2}\cap Z_{i}|=1
.

(iii)

After fixing the ends, we apply the corresponding lemma from Section

(i.e. Lemma

,
6.14
,
6.15
,
6.16
, and
6.17
) to construct the desired switching path, denoted by
P_{Z_{i}}
, which connects these endpoints while avoiding the set
B_{i}
.

(iv)

After constructing the switching path, we perform the operation
\mathrm{Ope}_{Z_{i}}
on
P_{Z_{i}}
, resulting in a new path, denoted by
\hat{P}_{Z_{i}}
(refer to the configurations shown on the right in Figures

,

,
41
,
42
,
43
, and
44
).

(v)

The set

is obtained from

and adding all
K_{4}
’s on
\hat{P}_{Z_{i}}
.
For example, if

and add

along with the two new

).

Note that for all six possible operations, there is exact one end
Q_{j}
that is no longer contained in

(and also

). The set

is obtained from

by removing this
Q_{j}
.
The set
\mathcal{S}_{i+1}
is obtained from
\mathcal{S}_{i}
by removing all members that have nonempty intersection with
Q_{j}
and adding all members in
\mathcal{K}_{i+1}
that have nonempty intersection with the other ends. For example, if

and

.

We repeat this process until
Z_{i}
is of type
444
and assume that the process terminate after
t
steps (so
t
is the index at which the process terminates).
From the definition of the process above, it is clear that
\mathrm{length}(Z_{i+1})=\mathrm{length}(Z_{i})-1
. Therefore, the process terminates after at most
12-3=9
steps.

The following claim follows from the definition of the process.

Claim 6.19
.

The following statements hold.

(i)

|\mathcal{A}_{6}|+|\mathcal{S}|=|\mathcal{K}_{0}|=\cdots=|\mathcal{K}_{t}|
,

(ii)

V(\mathcal{A}_{6}\cup\mathcal{S})=V(\mathcal{K}_{0})=\cdots=V(\mathcal{K}_{t})
,

(iii)

\mathcal{K}_{i}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{i}
is a
K_{4}
-tiling in
\mathcal{G}
for every
i\in[0,t]
,

(iv)

|\mathcal{Z}_{i}|=\mathrm{length}(Z_{i})
for every
i\in[0,t]
,

(v)

|B_{0}|\leq 12+12\cdot 4=60
and, for every
i\in[t-1]
, since each switching path uses at most
18
copies of
K_{4}
, we have

\displaystyle|B_{i+1}|\leq|B_{i}|+18\cdot 4=|B_{i}|+52.

Consequently,

\displaystyle|B_{i}|\leq|B_{0}|+52t\leq 60+52\cdot 12=684\quad\text{for every}\quad i\in[0,t].

By Claim
6.19
, we can choose
\mu
to be sufficiently large at the beginning to ensure that in Step
(iii)
, we can always apply Lemma
6.13
,
6.14
,
6.15
,
6.16
, and
6.17
.

By Claim
6.19

(i)
,
(ii)
,
(iii)
and
(iv)
,
\mathcal{A}_{1}\cup\cdots\cup\mathcal{A}_{5}\cup(\mathcal{K}_{t}\setminus\mathcal{Z}_{t})
is a
K_{4}
-tiling in
G
of size

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[5]}|\mathcal{A}_{i}|+|\mathcal{A}_{6}|+|\mathcal{S}|-|\mathcal{Z}_{t}|=|\mathcal{A}|+4-3=|\mathcal{A}|+1,

contradicting the maximality of
(|\mathcal{A}|,|\mathcal{B}|,|\mathcal{C}|,|\mathcal{D}|)
(in particular, the maximality of
|\mathcal{A}|
). Therefore, Case 1 cannot happen.
