7
Global estimation

In Sections
7.1
,
7.2
,
7.3
, and
7.4
, we define and solve several quadratic optimization problems.
In Section
7.5
, we present the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

For the moment, let us set aside the combinatorial meaning of
(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d)
and
(n,k)
in Sections
7.1
,
7.2
,
7.3
, and
7.4
, and consider them simply as undetermined variables (or real numbers).

7.1
Definitions and preliminaries

Given real numbers
n,k
satisfying
n\geq 4k\geq 0
, define the region

\displaystyle\Omega_{n,k}\coloneqq

\displaystyle\Bigl\{(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{9}\colon

\displaystyle\qquad a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6}=k\quad\text{and}\quad 4k+3b+2c+d=n\Bigr\}.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{9}
.
Recall that
\Psi(b,c,d)=3b^{2}+4bc+2bd+c^{2}+cd
was defined in Fact
7.2
.
Define

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{13a_{1}^{2}}{2}+13a_{1}(a_{2}+\cdots+a_{6})+7a_{2}^{2}+14a_{2}(a_{3}+\cdots+a_{6})

\displaystyle\qquad+7a_{3}^{2}+15a_{3}(a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6})+\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+15a_{4}(a_{5}+a_{6})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{15a_{5}^{2}}{2}+15a_{5}a_{6}+8a_{6}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+(9b+6c+3d)a_{1}+(8b+6c+3d)a_{2}+(8b+5c+3d)a_{3}

\displaystyle\qquad+(7b+5c+3d)a_{4}+(7b+5c+2d)a_{5}+(7b+5c+2d)a_{6},

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\Psi(b,c,d),

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{a_{6}^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{(b+c)a_{6}}{2},

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-(7b+5c+2d)a_{6}+(3b+2c+d)^{2}.

Define

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}g_{1/2}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/2}(a_{4},c),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\leq\frac{b+c}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\hat{g}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/4}(a_{4},c),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\geq\frac{b+c}{2},\end{cases}\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\hat{\varphi}(a_{4},b,c,d)-\left(\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}\right).

Define, for each
i\in[3]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{2,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\Phi_{4,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{2,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{(b+c)a_{4}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}+c^{2}}{4},

\displaystyle\Phi_{5,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{2,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{9ba_{4}}{10}-\frac{ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}.

Define

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{(b+c)a_{4}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}+c^{2}}{4},

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{9ba_{4}}{10}-\frac{ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}.

The following facts follow directly from the definition (recall the definition of
\Xi(n,k)
from Theorem
1.3
).

Fact 7.1
.

For every
(n,k)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{2}
satisfying
n\geq 4k
, we have

\displaystyle\Xi(n,k)\geq\max\left\{\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{kn}{3}-\frac{k^{2}}{6},~\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2},~3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}\right\}.

Fact 7.2
.

For every
(b,c,d)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\Psi(b,c,d)=\frac{(3b+2c+d)^{2}}{3}-\frac{c^{2}+cd+d^{2}}{3}\leq\frac{(3b+2c+d)^{2}}{3}.

Fact 7.3
.

For every
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{9}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),~\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\right\}\leq\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),~\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\right\}\leq\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d).

Proposition 7.4
.

For every
\alpha\in(0,1)
and for every
(a_{4},b,c)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{g}(a_{4},b)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{45a_{4}^{2}}{8}+\frac{61ba_{4}}{10}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150},~\frac{45a_{4}^{2}}{8}+7ba_{4}\right\},

(34)

\displaystyle g_{\alpha}(a_{4},b)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{15\alpha a_{4}^{2}}{2}+\frac{13ba_{4}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{8\alpha},~\frac{15\alpha a_{4}^{2}}{2}+7ba_{4}\right\},

(35)

\displaystyle\quad\text{and}\quad h_{\alpha}(a_{4},c)

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{15\alpha a_{4}^{2}}{2}+\frac{9ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{8\alpha},~\frac{15\alpha a_{4}^{2}}{2}+5ca_{4}\right\}.

(36)

Consequently, for every
i\in[3]
,

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),\quad\text{and}\quad

(37)

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{4,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\leq\frac{b+c}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{5,i}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\geq\frac{b+c}{2},\end{cases}

(38)

and

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{6,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\leq\frac{b+c}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{6,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}.\end{cases}

(39)

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

Inequalities (
34
), (
35
), and (
36
) follow from elementary (yet somewhat tedious) calculations, so we omit the details here.

It follows from (
35
) and (
36
) that

\displaystyle g_{1/2}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/2}(a_{4},c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{4}+\frac{13ba_{4}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}}{4}+\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{4}+\frac{9ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}-\frac{(b+c)a_{4}}{2}+\frac{b^{2}+c^{2}}{4},

(40)

and

\displaystyle g_{1/2}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/2}(a_{4},c)\leq\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{4}+7ba_{4}+\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{4}+5ca_{4}=\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}.

(41)

It follows from (
34
) and (
35
) that

\displaystyle\hat{g}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/4}(a_{4},c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{45a_{4}^{2}}{8}+\frac{61ba_{4}}{10}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150}+\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{8}+\frac{9ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{c^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}-\frac{9ba_{4}}{10}-\frac{ca_{4}}{2}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150}+\frac{c^{2}}{2},

(42)

and

\displaystyle\hat{g}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/4}(a_{4},c)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{45a_{4}^{2}}{8}+7ba_{4}+\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{8}+5ca_{4}=\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}.

(43)

Now, Inequalities (
38
) and (
39
) follow from (
40
), (
42
), and the definition.
Inequality (
37
) follows from (
41
), (
43
), and the definition.

Remark.

Notice from Inequalities (
41
) and (
43
) that

\displaystyle\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)\leq 0.

(44)

Let
I\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be an interval.
Recall that a function
f(x)\colon I\to\mathbb{R}
is
convex
if for every
\alpha\in[0,1]
and for all
x,y\in I
,

\displaystyle f\left(\alpha x+(1-\alpha)y\right)\leq\alpha\cdot f(x)+(1-\alpha)\cdot f(y).

Note that, in particular, a quadratic polynomial
q(x)
with a non-negative coefficient for
x^{2}
is convex.

Proposition 7.5
.

Let
(b,c)\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}^{2}
.
Suppose that
\gamma_{1}\geq-7,\gamma_{2}\in\mathbb{R},\gamma_{3}\in\mathbb{R}
are real numbers.
Then both

\displaystyle\rho_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{1}x^{2}+\gamma_{2}x+\gamma_{3}+g_{1/2}(x,b)+h_{1/2}(x,c)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\rho_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{1}x^{2}+\gamma_{2}x+\gamma_{3}+\hat{g}(x,b)+h_{1/4}(x,c)

are convex functions on
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
.

Proof of Proposition
7.5
.

First, let us consider
\rho_{1}(x)
.
We write
\rho_{1}(x)
as the summation of three functions
f_{1}(x)
,
f_{2}(x)
,
f_{3}(x)
, where

\displaystyle f_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{1}x^{2}+\gamma_{2}x+\gamma_{3}+7x^{2},

\displaystyle f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq g_{1/2}(x,b)-\frac{7x^{2}}{2},

\displaystyle f_{3}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq h_{1/2}(x,c)-\frac{7x^{2}}{2}.

Note that
f_{1}(x)
is a quadratic polynomial in
x
and the coefficient of
x^{2}
is
\gamma_{1}+7\geq 0
. So
f_{1}(x)
is convex.
Observe that both
f_{2}(x)
and
f_{3}(x)
are continuous and straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{2}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}=\begin{cases}7b,&\quad\text{if}\quad x<b,\\[5.0pt]
\frac{x+13b}{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq b,\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{3}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}=\begin{cases}5c,&\quad\text{if}\quad x<c,\\[5.0pt]
\frac{x+9c}{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq c.\end{cases}

Observe that both
\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{2}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{3}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}
are non-decreasing. So both
f_{2}(x)
and
f_{3}(x)
are convex.
Since the summation of convex functions is also convex,
\rho_{1}(x)
is convex.

Next, we consider
\rho_{2}(x)
.
We write
\rho_{2}(x)
as the summation of three functions
f_{1}(x)
,
f_{2}(x)
,
f_{3}(x)
, where

\displaystyle f_{1}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\gamma_{1}x^{2}+\gamma_{2}x+\gamma_{3}+7x^{2},

\displaystyle f_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\hat{g}(x,b)-\frac{21x^{2}}{4},

\displaystyle f_{3}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq h_{1/4}(x,c)-\frac{7x^{2}}{4}.

The proof of Case 1 shows that
f_{1}(x)
is convex.
Observe that both
f_{2}(x)
and
f_{3}(x)
are continuous and straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{2}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}7b,&\quad\text{if}\quad x<\frac{6b}{5},\\[5.0pt]
\frac{3x}{4}+\frac{61b}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\in\left[\frac{6b}{5},\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{7x}{10}+\frac{479b}{75},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\in\left[\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15},\frac{56b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{21x}{29}+\frac{913b}{145},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\in\left[\frac{56b}{15},\frac{38b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{3x}{4}+\frac{61b}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq\frac{38b}{5},\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{3}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}5c,&\quad\text{if}\quad x<2c,\\[5.0pt]
\frac{x+18c}{4},&\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq 2c.\end{cases}

Observe that both
\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{2}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}
and
\frac{\mathrm{d}f_{3}(x)}{\mathrm{d}x}
are non-decreasing. So both
f_{2}(x)
and
f_{3}(x)
are convex.
It follows that
\rho_{2}(x)
is convex as well.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.5
.

The following simple but useful fact will be used extensively in the remainder of the proof.

Fact 7.6
.

Let
[x_{1},x_{2}]\subseteq\mathbb{R}
be an interval. Suppose that
q(x)\colon[x_{1},x_{2}]\to\mathbb{R}
is convex. Then, for every
x\in[x_{1},x_{2}]
,

\displaystyle q(x)\leq\max\left\{q(x_{1}),~q(x_{2})\right\}.

In the remainder of this section, we assume that
n\geq 4k\geq 0
.

7.2
Inequalities for
\Phi
and
\Phi_{1}

In this subsection, we consider the optimization problem of maximizing
\Phi_{1}
over the region
\Omega_{n,k}
.

Proposition 7.7
.

For every
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big\{\Phi(a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4},0,0,0,a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Phi(0,a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4},0,0,a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\Phi\left(0,0,\hat{a}_{3},\hat{a}_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)\Big\},

where

\displaystyle(\hat{a}_{3},\hat{a}_{4})\coloneqq\left(\left(1+\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{a_{3}+a_{4}}\right)a_{3},\left(1+\frac{a_{1}+a_{2}}{a_{3}+a_{4}}\right)a_{4}\right).

Remark.
Proposition
7.7
shows that when maximizing
\Phi
(and hence, also
\Phi_{1}
) over the region
\Omega_{n,k}
, it suffices to consider the cases where
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{4}=0
,
a_{1}=a_{3}=a_{4}=0
, and
a_{1}=a_{2}=0
.

Proof of Proposition
7.7
.

We may assume that
\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)
is maximized over
\Omega_{n,k}
.

Claim 7.8
.

We may assume that
a_{1}=0
or
a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.8
.

Suppose that
a_{1}>0
and
a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}>0
. Let

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi(a_{1}+(a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4})x,(1-x)a_{2},(1-x)a_{3},(1-x)a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d).

Note that
q_{1}(x)
as a quadratic polynomial in
x
over the interval
\left[\frac{-a_{1}}{a_{2}+a_{3}+a4},1\right]
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
in
q_{1}(x)
is

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+2a_{4}^{2}+2a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}\right)+4a_{3}a_{4}}{2}\geq 0.

So it follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{-a_{1}}{a_{2}+a_{3}+a4}\right),~q_{1}(1)\right\}.

This implies that we can assume either
a_{1}=0
or
a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}=0
.

Claim 7.9
.

Suppose that
a_{1}=0
. Then we may assume that
a_{2}=0
or
a_{3}+a_{4}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.9
.

Suppose that
a_{2}>0
and
a_{3}+a_{4}>0
. Let

\displaystyle q_{2}(x)\coloneqq\Phi(0,a_{2}+(a_{3}+a_{4})x,(1-x)a_{3},(1-x)a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d).

Note that
q_{2}(x)
as a quadratic polynomial in
x
over the interval
\left[\frac{-a_{2}}{a_{3}+a4},1\right]
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
in
q_{2}(x)
is

\displaystyle\frac{a_{4}^{2}+2a_{3}a_{4}}{2}\geq 0.

So it follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\Phi(0,a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{2}(0)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{q_{2}(1),~q_{2}\left(\frac{-a_{2}}{a_{3}+a4}\right)\right\},

This implies that we can assume either
a_{2}=0
or
a_{3}+a_{4}=0
.

Proposition
7.7
now follows directly from Claims
7.8
and
7.9
.

Proposition 7.10
.

The following statements hold for
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.

(i)

\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Phi(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,a_{5}+a_{6},b,c,d)
.

(ii)

If
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{8}\right]
, then
\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Phi(a_{1}+a_{6},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)
.

In particular, if
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{8}\right]
, then

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Phi(a_{1}+a_{5}+a_{6},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d).

(45)

Proof of Proposition
7.10
.

Proposition
7.10

(i)
follows directly from the fact that

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,a_{5}+a_{6},b,c,d)-\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=\frac{a_{5}^{2}}{2}+a_{5}a_{6}\geq 0.

Proposition
7.10

(ii)
follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\Phi(a_{1}+a_{6},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)-\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{6}}{2}\left(4b+2c+2d-2a_{2}-4a_{3}-4a_{4}-4a_{5}-3a_{6}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{a_{6}}{2}\left(3b+2c+d-4(a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6})\right)\geq\frac{a_{6}}{2}\left(n-4k-4k\right)\geq 0.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.10
.

Proposition 7.11
.

The following inequalities hold for
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.

(i)

Suppose that
a_{2}=\cdots=a_{6}=0
. Then
\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{kn}{3}-\frac{k^{2}}{6}
.

(ii)

Suppose that
a_{1}=a_{3}=\cdots=a_{6}=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right].\end{cases}

In particular,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{1}(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,d),~\Phi_{1}(0,k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,d)\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.11
.

Recall from the definition of
\Omega_{n,k}
that
d=n-4k-3b-2c
.

Suppose that
a_{2}=\cdots=a_{6}=0
. Then
a_{1}=k
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(a_{1},0,0,0,0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\frac{13a_{1}^{2}}{2}+3(3b+2c+d)a_{1}+\Psi(b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{13a_{1}^{2}}{2}+3(3b+2c+d)a_{1}+\frac{(3b+2c+d)^{2}}{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{13a_{1}^{2}}{2}+3(n-4a_{1})a_{1}+\frac{(n-4a_{1})^{2}}{3}=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{kn}{3}-\frac{k^{2}}{6}.

Suppose that
a_{1}=a_{3}=\cdots=a_{6}=0
. Then
a_{2}=k
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,a_{2},0,0,0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=7a_{2}^{2}+(8b+6c+3d)a_{2}+\Psi(b,c,d)

\displaystyle=-5k^{2}+3kn-3b^{2}-c^{2}-3bc+(n-4k)c+(2n-9k)b

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(x-\frac{n-6k}{3}\right)^{2}-\left(y-\frac{n-4k}{2}\right)^{2},

(46)

where
x\coloneqq b
and
y\coloneqq\frac{3b}{2}+c
.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right]
. Then it follows trivially from (
46
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,a_{2},0,0,0,0,b,c,d)\leq\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2}.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
. Then
\frac{n-6k}{3}\leq 0
and it follows from (
46
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,a_{2},0,0,0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(0-\frac{n-6k}{3}\right)^{2}-\left(y-\frac{n-4k}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2}-\left(y-\frac{n-4k}{2}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2}.

The “In particular” part follows from Fact
7.1
and the fact that

\displaystyle\Xi(n,k)\geq\begin{cases}\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right].\end{cases}

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.11
.

Proposition 7.12
.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]\cup\left[\frac{n}{5},\frac{n}{4}\right]
. Then

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)\colon(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

In particular, by (
45
), for
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{8}\right]
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\colon(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

(47)

Proof of Proposition
7.12
.

We may assume that
\Phi_{1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)
is maximized over the region
\left\{(w_{1},\ldots,w_{6},x,y,z)\in\Omega_{n,k}\colon w_{5}=w_{6}=0\right\}
.
By Proposition
7.7
, it suffices to consider the cases where
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{4}=0
,
a_{1}=a_{3}=a_{4}=0
, and
a_{1}=a_{2}=0
.
The first two cases follow from Proposition
7.11

(i)
and
(ii)
, respectively. So it suffices to consider the case where
a_{1}=a_{2}=0
.

Case 1
:
b\geq k
(Since
b\leq\frac{n-4k}{3}
holds trivially, this case is possible only when
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{7}\right]
).

Note that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},k-a_{3},0,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}-\frac{\left(a_{3}-b\right)^{2}}{2}-\left(c-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}-\frac{\left(k-b\right)^{2}}{2}-\left(c-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}+2kn+5k^{2}-3b^{2}-6bk+2bn}{4}-\left(c-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}.

(48)

Case 1.1
:
b\in\left[\max\left\{k,\frac{n-5k}{3}\right\},\frac{n-4k}{3}\right]
.

Since
b\geq\frac{n-5k}{3}
, we have
\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\leq 0
.
It follows from (
48
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+5k^{2}-3b^{2}-6bk+2bn}{4}-\left(0-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{7k^{2}}{4}-3\left(b-\frac{2n-9k}{6}\right)^{2}.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{2n}{15}\right]
. Then
\frac{2n-9k}{3}\in\left[\max\left\{k,\frac{n-5k}{3}\right\},\frac{n-4k}{3}\right]
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{7k^{2}}{4}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{2n}{15},\frac{n}{7}\right]
. Then
\frac{2n-9k}{3}\leq k
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{7k^{2}}{4}-3\left(k-\frac{2n-9k}{6}\right)^{2}=5kn-17k^{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Case 1.2
:
b\in\left[k,~\max\left\{k,\frac{n-5k}{3}\right\}\right]
.

It follows from (
48
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+5k^{2}-3b^{2}-6bk+2bn}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{n-3k}{3}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+2k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{n-5k}{3}-\frac{n-3k}{3}\right)^{2}=\frac{n^{2}}{3}+\frac{5k^{2}}{3}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Case 2
:
b\leq k
.

Similar to the proof of (
48
), we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}-\frac{\left(a_{3}-b\right)^{2}}{2}-\left(c-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}-\left(c-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}.

(49)

Case 2.1
:
b\in\left[\frac{n-5k}{3},~\min\left\{k,\frac{n-4k}{3}\right\}\right]
(this case is possible only when
k\in\left[\frac{n}{8},\frac{n}{4}\right]
, since we need
\frac{n-5k}{3}\leq k
).

Since
b\geq\frac{n-5k}{3}
, we have
\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\leq 0
.
It follows from (
49
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}-\left(0-\frac{n-5k-3b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2n^{2}}{5}-kn+\frac{11k^{2}}{2}-\frac{5}{2}\left(b-\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\right)^{2}.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{8},~\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\frac{2n^{2}}{5}-kn+\frac{11k^{2}}{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{5},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.
Then
\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\leq 0
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2n^{2}}{5}-kn+\frac{11k^{2}}{2}-\frac{5}{2}\left(0-\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\right)^{2}=3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Case 2.2
:
b\in\left[0,~\min\left\{k,\frac{n-5k}{3}\right\}\right]
(this case is possible only when
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{5}\right]
, since we need
0\leq\frac{n-5k}{3}
).

By assumption,
k
lies in the interval
\left[0,~\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]
.
It follows from (
49
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}+2kn+7k^{2}}{2}+\frac{-b^{2}+2bn-10bk}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn+8k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left(b-(n-5k)\right)^{2}.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{8}\right]
. Then
k\leq\frac{n-5k}{3}\leq n-5k
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn+8k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left(k-(n-5k)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{4}+kn-k^{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{8},~\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]
. Then
\frac{n-5k}{3}\leq k
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn+8k^{2}-\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{n-5k}{3}-(n-5k)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{18}\left(7n^{2}-16kn+94k^{2}\right)\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.12
.

7.3
Inequalities for
\Phi_{2,i}

In this subsection, we consider the optimization problem of maximizing
\Phi_{2,i}
for
i\in[3]
over the region
\Omega_{n,k}
.

Proposition 7.13
.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]\cup\left[\frac{n}{5},\frac{n}{4}\right]
. Then

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\colon(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.13
.

Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6},0,0,b,c,d)-(a_{5}+a_{6})d

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.12
.

Proposition 7.14
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
. The following statements hold.

(i)

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right]
and
a_{6}=k
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

(ii)

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d\right)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.14
.

Recall that
\frac{n-4k}{2}=\frac{3b+2c+d}{2}
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{(b+c)a_{6}}{2}.

Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right]
and
a_{6}=k
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}(0,\ldots,0,k,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)

\displaystyle=\frac{4n^{2}-6kn+57k^{2}}{12}-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{2n-9k}{6}\right)^{2}-\left(c-\frac{2n-7k-6b}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4n^{2}-6kn+57k^{2}}{12}\leq\Xi(n,k),

which proves Proposition
7.14

(i)
.

Next, we prove Proposition
7.14

(ii)
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.

Claim 7.15
.

We may assume that
a_{1}=0
or
a_{1}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Proof of Claim
7.15
.

Suppose that
0<a_{1}<k-a_{6}
. Then
a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}>0
.
Let

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{2,2}\left(a_{1}+(a_{2}+\cdots+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{2},\ldots,(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right).

Note that
q_{1}(x)
is a quadratic polynomial in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
in
q_{1}(x)
is

\displaystyle\frac{a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+2\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)^{2}+2\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)a_{2}+4a_{3}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)}{2}\geq 0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{-a_{1}}{a_{2}+\cdots+a_{5}}\right),~q_{1}\left(1\right)\right\},

which means that we may assume either
a_{1}=0
or
a_{1}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Suppose that
a_{1}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.
Then
a_{2}+\cdots+a_{5}=k-(a_{1}+a_{6})=0
and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(3k-\frac{n}{2},0,\ldots,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)-\frac{(n-4k)(b+d)}{4}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,2}\left(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}\left(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.11

(i)
.

Therefore, it suffices to consider the case
a_{1}=0
.

Claim 7.16
.

We may assume that
a_{2}=0
or
a_{2}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Proof of Claim
7.16
.

The proof is similar to that of Claim
7.15
.
Let

\displaystyle q_{2}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},a_{2}+(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{3},(1-x)a_{4},(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d).

Note that
q_{2}(x)
is a quadratic polynomial in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
in
q_{2}(x)
is

\displaystyle\frac{\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\left(2a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)}{2}\geq 0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{2}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{2}\left(\frac{-a_{2}}{a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}}\right),~q_{2}\left(1\right)\right\},

which means that we may assume either
a_{2}=0
or
a_{2}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Suppose that
a_{2}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.
Then
a_{1}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}=k-(a_{2}+a_{6})=0
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,k,0\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)-\frac{(n-4k)(c+d)}{4}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,k,0\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}\left(0,k,0\ldots,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.11

(ii)
.

So it suffices to consider the case
a_{2}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{1}=0
).
Note that, in this case, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,a_{6},b,c,d\right)-da_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,a_{6},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,a_{3},3k-\frac{n}{2}-a_{3},0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right),&\quad\text{if}\quad b\geq 3k-\frac{n}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right),&\quad\text{if}\quad b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2},\end{cases}

(50)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
a_{3}\in\left[0,3k-\frac{n}{2}\right]
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,a_{3},3k-\frac{n}{2}-a_{3},0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(a_{3}-b\right)^{2}}{2}+\frac{-20b^{2}-24bc-120bk+26bn-8c^{2}-64ck+14cn-84k^{2}+48kn-3n^{2}}{8}.

By (
50
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Xi(n,k)\geq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast},&\quad\text{if}\quad b\geq 3k-\frac{n}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle},&\quad\text{if}\quad b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2}.\end{cases}

Case 1
:
b\geq 3k-\frac{n}{2}
(this case is possible only when
k\in\left[0,\frac{5n}{26}\right]
, since
b\leq\frac{n-4k}{3}
holds trivially).

It follows from the assumption that
k
lies in the interval
\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{5n}{26}\right]
.
Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{-48b^{2}+8bn+64k^{2}+32kn+17n^{2}}{64}-\left(c-\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8}\right)^{2}.

(51)

Recall that
c
lies in the interval
\left[0,\frac{n-4k-3b}{2}\right]
.
Since
\frac{n-4k-3b}{2}\leq\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8}
iff
k\leq\frac{3n}{16}
, it follows from (
51
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,\frac{n-4k-3b}{2},d\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast\ast},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{3n}{16}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8},d\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast\blacktriangle},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{5n}{26}\right].\end{cases}

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{3n}{16}\right]
, then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast}\leq\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{25n^{2}}{192}+2kn-3k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{n}{12}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{25n^{2}}{192}+2kn-3k^{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{5n}{26}\right]
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast}\leq\Phi_{2,2}^{\ast\blacktriangle}=\frac{13n^{2}}{48}+\frac{kn}{2}+k^{2}-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{n}{12}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{13n^{2}}{48}+\frac{kn}{2}+k^{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Case 2
:
b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{-16b^{2}-192bk+40bn+352k^{2}-64kn+25n^{2}}{64}-\left(c-\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8}\right)^{2}.

Note that
\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8}\leq 0
if
k\geq\frac{7n}{32}
, and
c\leq\frac{n-4k-3b}{2}\leq\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8}
if
k\geq\frac{3n}{16}
, so we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\ast},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{7n}{32},\frac{n}{4}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,\frac{7n-32k-12b}{8},d\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\blacktriangle},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{7n}{32}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,\frac{n-4k-3b}{2},d\right)=:\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\blacktriangledown},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{3n}{16}\right].\end{cases}

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{7n}{32},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.
Then
\frac{13n-60k}{20}\leq 0
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{109n^{2}}{160}-\frac{15kn}{4}+12k^{2}-\frac{5}{2}\left(b-\frac{13n-60k}{20}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{109n^{2}}{160}-\frac{15kn}{4}+12k^{2}-\frac{5}{2}\left(0-\frac{13n-60k}{20}\right)^{2}=\frac{-3(n^{2}-16kn+28k^{2})}{8}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{7n}{32}\right]
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\blacktriangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{25n^{2}-152kn+464k^{2}}{32}-\frac{1}{4}\left(b-\frac{5n-24k}{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{25n^{2}}{64}-kn+\frac{11k^{2}}{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{5n}{24},\frac{7n}{32}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{25n^{2}-152kn+464k^{2}}{32},&\quad\text{if}\quad k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{5n}{24}\right],\end{cases}

which is smaller than
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{3n}{16},\frac{7n}{32}\right]
.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{3n}{16}\right]
.
Then
b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2}\leq\frac{5n-24k}{4}
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}^{\blacktriangle\blacktriangledown}

\displaystyle=\frac{41n^{2}-208kn+672k^{2}}{64}-\frac{1}{4}\left(b-\frac{5n-24k}{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{41n^{2}-208kn+672k^{2}}{64}-\frac{1}{4}\left(3k-\frac{n}{2}-\frac{5n-24k}{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{-n^{2}+37kn-78k^{2}}{8}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.14
.

Proposition 7.17
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[0,\frac{(20+\sqrt{30})n}{130}\right]\cup\left[\frac{n}{5},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}\leq\frac{n-4k}{2}
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.17
.

Let
a_{6}^{\ast}\coloneqq\min\left\{\frac{n-4k}{2},k\right\}
. Note the
a_{6}
lies in the interval
[0,a_{6}^{\ast}]
.

Claim 7.18
.

We may assume that
a_{6}=0
or
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}
.

Proof of Claim
7.18
.

Note that

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{2,2}((1-x)a_{1},\ldots,(1-x)a_{5},a_{6}+(a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5})x,b,c,d).

is quadratic in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
in
q_{1}(x)
is

\displaystyle\frac{3a_{1}^{2}}{2}+\left(2a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)a_{1}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+\frac{\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\left(2a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)}{2}\geq 0.

So we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{-a_{6}}{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5}}\right),~q_{1}\left(\frac{a_{6}^{\ast}-a_{6}}{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5}}\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{6}=0
or
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}
.

Suppose that
a_{6}=0
.
Then straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}+a_{5},0,0,b,c,d)-da_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}+a_{5},0,0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{4}+a_{5},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.12
.

Suppose that
k\leq\frac{n-4k}{2}
(equivalently,
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{6}\right]
) and
a_{6}=k
.
Then it follows from Proposition
7.14

(i)
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
\frac{n-4k}{2}\leq k
(equivalently,
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
) and
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
Then it follows from Proposition
7.14

(ii)
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.17
.

Proposition 7.19
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}\in\left[\frac{n-4k}{2},k\right]
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.19
.

Recall that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=\Phi(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-(7b+5c+2d)a_{6}+(3b+2c+d)^{2}.

Claim 7.20
.

We may assume that
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
or
a_{6}=k
.

Proof of Claim
7.20
.

Suppose that
\frac{n-4k}{2}<a_{6}<k
.
Note that

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{2,3}((1-x)a_{1},\ldots,(1-x)a_{5},a_{6}+(a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5})x,b,c,d)

is quadratic in
x
and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is

\displaystyle\frac{3a_{1}^{2}}{2}+\left(2a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)a_{1}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)+\frac{\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\left(2a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)}{2}\geq 0.

So

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{(n-4k)/2-a_{6}}{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5}}\right),~q_{1}\left(\frac{k-a_{6}}{a_{1}+\cdots+a_{5}}\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
and
a_{6}=k
.

Case 1
:
a_{6}=k
.

We have
a_{1}=\cdots=a_{5}=k-a_{6}=0
and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=\Phi_{2,2}(0,\ldots,0,k,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)=n^{2}-8kn+24k^{2},

which is less or equal to
\Xi(n,k)
for all
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

Case 2
:
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.

Similar to Claim
7.15
, since the coefficient of
x^{2}
in

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}\left(a_{1}+(a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{2},\ldots,(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{2}^{2}+2\left(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)a_{2}+a_{3}^{2}+2\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right){}^{2}+4a_{3}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\right)\geq 0,

we may assume either
a_{1}=0
or
a_{1}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Suppose that
a_{1}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
. Then
a_{2}=\cdots=a_{5}=k-(a_{1}+a_{6})=0
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}\left(3k-\frac{n}{2},0,\ldots,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{n^{2}}{8}+4kn-\frac{15k^{2}}{2},

which is smaller than
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

So we may assume that
a_{1}=0
. Similar to Claim
7.16
,
since the coefficient of
x^{2}
in

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}\left(a_{1},a_{2}+(a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{3},\ldots,(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\left(2a_{3}+a_{4}+a_{5}\right)\geq 0,

we may assume that
a_{2}=0
or
a_{2}=k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
.

Suppose that
a_{2}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
. Then
a_{1}=a_{3}=a_{4}=a_{5}=k-(a_{2}+a_{6})=0
, and

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{n^{2}}{4}+5kn-9k^{2}-\frac{b(6k-n)}{2}\leq-\frac{n^{2}}{4}+5kn-9k^{2},

which is smaller than
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

So we may assume that
a_{2}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{1}=0
). Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,a_{3},3k-\frac{n}{2}-a_{3},0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\left(a_{3}-b\right)^{2}}{2}+\frac{4b^{2}-48bk+8bn-24ck+4cn-84k^{2}+48kn-3n^{2}}{8}.

Since
a_{3}\leq k-a_{6}=3k-\frac{n}{2}
, it follows from the inequality above that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,a_{6},b,c,d\right)-da_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,a_{6},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,a_{3},3k-\frac{n}{2}-a_{3},0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,3k-\frac{n}{2},0,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)=:\Phi_{2,3}^{\ast},&\quad\text{if}\quad b\geq 3k-\frac{n}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,3}\left(0,0,b,3k-\frac{n}{2}-b,0,\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,n-4k-3b-2c\right)=:\Phi_{2,3}^{\blacktriangle},&\quad\text{if}\quad b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2}.\end{cases}

Suppose that
b\geq 3k-\frac{n}{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(-n^{2}+15kn-30k^{2}-(b+c)(6k-n)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(-n^{2}+15kn-30k^{2}-\left(3k-\frac{n}{2}\right)(6k-n)\right)=\frac{3}{4}\left(-n^{2}+14kn-32k^{2}\right),

which is smaller than
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

Suppose that
b\leq 3k-\frac{n}{2}=\frac{6k-n}{2}\leq 6k-n
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{2,3}^{\blacktriangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{-7n^{2}+96kn-228k^{2}}{8}+\frac{\left(b-(6k-n)\right)^{2}}{2}-\frac{c(6k-n)}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{-7n^{2}+96kn-228k^{2}}{8}+\frac{\left(0-(6k-n)\right)^{2}}{2}=\frac{3}{8}\left(-n^{2}+16kn-28k^{2}\right),

which is smaller than
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.19
.

7.4
Inequalities for
\Phi_{3,i}
and others

In this subsection, we consider the optimization problem for
\Phi_{3,i}
and other functions defined in Section
7.1
.

Proposition 7.21
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{4}=a_{6}=0
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.21
.

Let
\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}\coloneqq\Phi_{6,1}(0,0,a_{3},0,k-a_{3},0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)
.
Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3b^{2}}{4}+\frac{5c^{2}}{4}+bc+bk+5ck-cn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn+\frac{15k^{2}}{2}-\frac{\left(a_{3}+2b+2c+4k-n\right)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{3b^{2}}{4}+\frac{5c^{2}}{4}+bc+bk+5ck-cn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn+\frac{15k^{2}}{2}=:q(b,c).

Let us consider
q_{1}(x)\coloneqq q\left(b+\frac{x}{3},c-\frac{x}{2}\right)
, where we use
\frac{x}{3}
and
-\frac{x}{2}
because we need
3\left(b+\frac{x}{3}\right)+2\left(c-\frac{x}{2}\right)
to be a constant. Note that
q_{1}(x)
is quadratic in
x
and simple calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is
\frac{1}{16}>0
. Thus we have

\displaystyle q(b,c)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(-3b\right),~q_{1}\left(2c\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
b=0
or
c=0
.

Suppose that
b=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{5}{4}\left(c-\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\right)^{2}+\frac{5k^{2}}{2}+\frac{3n^{2}}{10}-\frac{\left(a_{3}+2c+4k-n\right)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{4}\left(c-\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\right)^{2}+\frac{5k^{2}}{2}+\frac{3n^{2}}{10}.

Notice that
c
lies in the interval
\left[0,\frac{n-4k}{2}\right]
.
Since
k\geq\frac{n}{6}
, we have
\frac{n-4k}{2}\geq 2\cdot\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}
. Thus, the inequality above continues as

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{4}\left(\frac{n-4k}{2}-\frac{2(n-5k)}{5}\right)^{2}+\frac{5k^{2}}{2}+\frac{3n^{2}}{10}=\frac{5n^{2}}{16}+\frac{5k^{2}}{2},

which is smaller that
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

Suppose that
c=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{2k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{47k^{2}}{6}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-\frac{\left(a_{3}+2b+4k-n\right)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{3}{4}\left(b-\frac{2k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{47k^{2}}{6}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}.

Notice that
b
lies in the interval
\left[0,\frac{n-4k}{3}\right]
. Since
k\geq\frac{n}{6}
, we have
\frac{n-4k}{3}\leq\frac{2k}{3}
. Thus, the inequality above continues as

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,1}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{n-4k}{3}-\frac{2k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{47k^{2}}{6}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}=\frac{29k^{2}}{6}-kn+\frac{5n^{2}}{12},

which is smaller that
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.21
.

Proposition 7.22
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
,
d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}
, and
a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{4}=a_{6}=0
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.22
.

Combining
d=n-4k-3b-2c
with
d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}
, we obtain
7b+5c\leq 2n-8k
, which implies that
b\leq\frac{2n-8k}{7}
and
c\leq\frac{2n-8k}{5}
.

Let
\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}\coloneqq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},0,k-a_{3},0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)
.
Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{2}}{150}+bc+bk+\frac{3c^{2}}{2}+5ck-cn+\frac{15k^{2}}{2}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}-\frac{\left(a_{3}+2b+2c+4k-n\right)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b^{2}}{150}+bc+bk+\frac{3c^{2}}{2}+5ck-cn+\frac{15k^{2}}{2}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}=:q(b,c).

Let us consider
q_{1}(x)\coloneqq q\left(b+\frac{x}{3},c-\frac{x}{2}\right)
. Note that
q_{1}(x)
is quadratic in
x
and simple calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is
\frac{1129}{5400}>0
. Thus we have

\displaystyle q(b,c)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(-3b\right),q_{1}\left(2c\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
b=0
or
c=0
.

Suppose that
b=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{2}\left(c-\frac{n-5k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{\left(n^{2}-kn+10k^{2}\right)}{3}-\frac{(a_{3}+2c+4k-n)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(c-\frac{n-5k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{\left(n^{2}-kn+10k^{2}\right)}{3}.

Recall that
c
is contained in the interval
\left[0,\frac{2n-8k}{5}\right]
.
Since
k\geq\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278}\geq\frac{2n}{13}
, we have
\frac{2n-8k}{5}\geq 2\cdot\frac{n-5k}{3}
. Thus, the inequality above continues as

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\left(\frac{2n-8k}{5}-\frac{n-5k}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\left(10k^{2}-kn+n^{2}\right)=\frac{1}{50}\left(167k^{2}-16kn+17n^{2}\right),

which is smaller that
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

Suppose that
c=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn-30k^{2}+\frac{(b+75k)^{2}}{150}-\frac{(a_{3}+2b+4k-n)^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n^{2}}{2}-2kn-30k^{2}+\frac{(b+75k)^{2}}{150}.

Recall that
b
lies in the interval
\left[0,\frac{2n-8k}{7}\right]
. Thus, the inequality above continues as

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{150}\left(\frac{2n-8k}{7}+75k\right)^{2}-30k^{2}-2kn+\frac{n^{2}}{2}=\frac{46789k^{2}-12632kn+3679n^{2}}{7350},

which is smaller that
\Xi(n,k)
for
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

Proposition 7.23
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{(24086-35\sqrt{3282})n}{128054},\frac{n}{4}\right]
,
d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}
, and
a_{1}=a_{2}=a_{5}=a_{6}=0
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.23
.

Combining
d=n-4k-3b-2c
with
d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}
, we obtain
7b+5c\leq 2n-8k
, which implies that
b\leq\frac{2n-8k}{7}
and
c\leq\frac{2n-8k}{5}
.

Let
\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}\coloneqq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},k-a_{3},0,0,b,c,n-4k-3b-2c)
. Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}-\frac{113b^{2}}{600}-\frac{41bc}{20}-\frac{109bk}{10}+2bn-\frac{3c^{2}}{8}-\frac{11ck}{2}+cn-\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{3}-\frac{19b+5c}{10}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}-\frac{113b^{2}}{600}-\frac{41bc}{20}-\frac{109bk}{10}+2bn-\frac{3c^{2}}{8}-\frac{11ck}{2}+cn=:q(b,c).

Let us consider
q_{1}(x)\coloneqq q\left(b+\frac{x}{3},c-\frac{x}{2}\right)
. Note that
q_{1}(x)
is quadratic in
x
and simple calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is
\frac{4903}{21600}>0
. Thus we have

\displaystyle q(b,c)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}(-3b),~q_{1}(2c)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
b=0
or
c=0
.

Suppose that
b=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(2n^{2}-13kn+47k^{2}\right)}{3}-\frac{3}{8}\left(c-\frac{2(2n-11k)}{3}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{3}-\frac{c}{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\left(47k^{2}-13kn+2n^{2}\right)}{3}-\frac{3}{8}\left(c-\frac{2(2n-11k)}{3}\right)^{2}.

If
k\in\left[\frac{(24086-35\sqrt{3282})n}{128054},\frac{2n}{11}\right]
, then we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}\leq\frac{\left(47k^{2}-13kn+2n^{2}\right)}{3}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

If
k\in\left[\frac{2n}{11},\frac{n}{4}\right]
, then
\frac{2(2n-11k)}{3}\leq 0
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}\leq\frac{\left(47k^{2}-13kn+2n^{2}\right)}{3}-\frac{3}{8}\left(0-\frac{2(2n-11k)}{3}\right)^{2}=3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

Suppose that
c=0
. Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle=\frac{3\left(200n^{2}-2067kn+5771k^{2}\right)}{113}-\frac{113}{600}\left(b-\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{3}-\frac{19b}{10}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\left(200n^{2}-2067kn+5771k^{2}\right)}{113}-\frac{113}{600}\left(b-\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}\right)^{2}.

Recall that
b
lies in the interval
\left[0,\frac{2n-8k}{7}\right]
.

If
k\in\left[\frac{20n}{109},\frac{n}{4}\right]
, then
\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}\leq 0
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\left(200n^{2}-2067kn+5771k^{2}\right)}{113}-\frac{113}{600}\left(0-\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=3kn-\frac{9k^{2}}{2}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

If
k\in\left[\frac{1987n}{10993},\frac{20n}{109}\right]
, then trivially,

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\left(200n^{2}-2067kn+5771k^{2}\right)}{113}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.

If
k\in\left[\frac{(24086-35\sqrt{3282})n}{128054},\frac{1987n}{10993}\right]
, then
\frac{2n-8k}{7}\leq\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}
, and hence,

\displaystyle\Phi_{6,2}^{\ast}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\left(200n^{2}-2067kn+5771k^{2}\right)}{113}-\frac{113}{600}\left(\frac{2n-8k}{7}-\frac{30(20n-109k)}{113}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4087n^{2}-16736kn+56677k^{2}}{7350}\leq\Xi(n,k),

as desired.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.23
.

Proposition 7.24
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}=0
. Then

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),~\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.24
.

By Fact
7.3
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Since
a_{6}=0
, all terms involving
a_{6}
in
\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)
vanish.

Claim 7.25
.

We may assume that
a_{1}=0
or
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{5}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.25
.

Suppose that
a_{1}>0
and
a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}>0
.
Notice that

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{6}\left(a_{1}+(a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{2},(1-x)a_{3},a_{4},(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

is a quadratic polynomial in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(a_{2}^{2}+a_{3}^{2}+2a_{5}^{2}+2\left(a_{3}+a_{5}\right)a_{2}+4a_{3}a_{5}\right)>0.

So it follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{-a_{1}}{a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}}\right),~q_{1}(1)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{1}=0
or
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{5}=0
.

Claim 7.26
.

Suppose that
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{5}=0
. Then we may assume that
a_{1}=0
or
a_{4}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.26
.

Let
\Sigma_{1,4}\coloneqq a_{1}+a_{4}
and consider

\displaystyle q_{2}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{6}(\Sigma_{1,4}-x,0,0,x,0,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=-\frac{13x^{2}}{2}+(2a_{6}-9b-6c)x+\hat{\varphi}(x,b,c,d)+A,

where
A
is independent of
x
.
It follows from Proposition
7.5
that
q_{2}(x)
is a convex function in
x
. So, by Fact
7.6
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},0,0,a_{4},0,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{2}(a_{4})\leq\max\left\{q_{2}(0),~q_{2}(\Sigma_{1,4})\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{1}=0
or
a_{4}=0
.

Suppose that
a_{2}=a_{3}=a_{5}=0
and
a_{4}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{6}=0
). Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{6}(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,d)=\Phi_{1}(k,0,\ldots,0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.11
.

So it suffices to consider the case
a_{1}=0
.

Claim 7.27
.

Suppose that
a_{1}=0
. Then we may assume that
a_{2}=0
or
a_{3}=a_{5}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.27
.

Suppose that
a_{2}>0
and
a_{3}+a_{5}>0
.
Note that

\displaystyle q_{3}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{6}\left(0,a_{2}+(a_{3}+a_{5})x,(1-x)a_{3},a_{4},(1-x)a_{5},a_{6},b,c,d\right)

is a quadratic polynomial in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}a_{5}\left(2a_{3}+a_{5}\right)\geq 0.

So, it follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(0,a_{2},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{3}(x)\leq\max\left\{q_{3}\left(\frac{-a_{2}}{a_{3}+a_{5}}\right),~q_{3}(1)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{2}=0
or
a_{3}=a_{5}=0
.

Claim 7.28
.

Suppose that
a_{3}=a_{5}=0
. Then we may assume that
a_{2}=0
or
a_{4}=0
.

Proof of Claim
7.28
.

Suppose that
a_{3}=a_{5}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{1}=0
). Then let
\Sigma_{2,4}\coloneqq a_{2}+a_{4}
and consider

\displaystyle q_{4}(x)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{6}(0,\Sigma_{2,4}-x,0,x,0,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=-7x^{2}+(a_{6}-8b-6c)x+\hat{\varphi}(a_{4},b,c,d)+B,

where
B
is independent of
x
.
It follows from Proposition
7.5
that
q_{2}(x)
is a convex function in
x
. So,

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(0,a_{2},0,a_{4},0,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{4}(a_{4})\leq\max\left\{q_{4}(0),~q_{4}(\Sigma_{2,4})\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{2}=0
or
a_{4}=0
.

Suppose that
a_{1}=a_{3}=a_{4}=a_{5}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{6}=0
). Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{6}(0,k,0,0,0,0,b,c,d)=\Phi_{1}(0,k,0,0,0,0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k),

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.11
.

So it remains to consider the case
a_{1}=a_{2}=0
(recall that we are also assuming
a_{6}=0
).

Case 1
:
d\leq\frac{b+c}{2}
.

It follows from (
39
) and the assumption
d\leq\frac{b+c}{2}
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,1}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{6,1}(0,0,a_{3},0,a_{4}+a_{5},0,b,c,d)-\frac{a_{4}(b+c-2d)}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,1}(0,0,a_{3},0,a_{4}+a_{5},0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k)

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.21
.

Case 2
:
d\in\left[\frac{b+c}{2},\frac{9b+5c}{10}\right]
.

It follows from (
39
) and the assumption
d\in\left[\frac{b+c}{2},\frac{9b+5c}{10}\right]
that

\displaystyle Phi_{6}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},0,a_{4}+a_{5},0,b,c,d)-\frac{a_{4}(9b+5c-10d)}{10}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},0,a_{4}+a_{5},0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k)

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.22
.

Case 3
:
d\geq\frac{9b+5c}{10}
.

It follows from (
39
) and the assumption
d\geq\frac{9b+5c}{10}\geq\frac{b+c}{2}
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{6}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4},a_{5},0,b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,0,b,c,d)-\frac{a_{4}(10d-9b-5c)}{10}

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{6,2}(0,0,a_{3},a_{4}+a_{5},0,0,b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k)

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.23
.

This completes the proof of Proposition
7.24
.

Proposition 7.29
.

Suppose that
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.
Then

\displaystyle\max\left\{\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\colon(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}\right\}\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.29
.

Fix
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d).

Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}+a_{6},0,b,c,d).

So it follows from Proposition
7.24
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k),

completing the proof of Proposition
7.29
.

Proposition 7.30
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose
k\in\left[\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}\leq\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.30
.

Recall that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\frac{(b+c)a_{6}}{2}+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d).

Let
a_{6}^{\ast}\coloneqq\min\left\{\frac{n-4k}{2},k-a_{4}\right\}
, noting that
a_{6}
lies in the interval
[0,a_{6}^{\ast}]
.

Claim 7.31
.

We may assume that
a_{6}=0
or
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}
.

Proof of Claim
7.31
.

Suppose that
0<a_{6}<a_{6}^{\ast}
.
Then
a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}>0
.
Notice that

\displaystyle q_{1}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{3,2}((1-x)a_{1},(1-x)a_{2},(1-x)a_{3},a_{4},(1-x)a_{5},a_{6}+(a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5})x,b,c,d)

is a quadratic polynomial in
x
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
x^{2}
is

\displaystyle\frac{3a_{1}^{2}}{2}+\left(2a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}\right)a_{1}+a_{2}^{2}+a_{2}\left(a_{3}+a_{5}\right)+\frac{1}{2}a_{5}\left(2a_{3}+a_{5}\right)\geq 0.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(\frac{-a_{6}}{a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}}\right),~q_{1}\left(\frac{a_{6}^{\ast}-a_{6}}{a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}}\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{6}=0
or
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}
.

By Proposition
7.24
, we are done if
a_{6}=0
. So it suffices to consider the case
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}
.

Claim 7.32
.

We may assume that
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.

Proof of Claim
7.32
.

Suppose that
k-a_{4}<\frac{n-4k}{2}
(equivalently,
a_{4}>k-\frac{n-4k}{2}
). Then
a_{6}=a_{6}^{\ast}=\min\left\{\frac{n-4k}{2},k-a_{4}\right\}=a_{6}^{\ast}=k-a_{4}
, meaning that
a_{1}+a_{2}+a_{3}+a_{5}=k-(a_{4}+a_{6})=0
.
Since

\displaystyle q_{2}(x)\coloneqq\Phi_{3,2}(0,0,0,x,0,k-x,b,c,d)=-7x^{2}-\left(k+\frac{13b+9c}{2}-d\right)x+\hat{\varphi}(x,b,c,d)+A,

where
A
is independent of
x
, it follows from Proposition
7.5
that
q_{2}(x)
is a convex function in
x
.
Thus, by Fact
7.6
, we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,2}(0,0,0,a_{4},0,a_{6},b,c,d)=q_{2}(a_{4})\leq\max\left\{q_{2}(k),~q_{2}\left(k-\frac{n-4k}{2}\right)\right\},

meaning that we can assume either
a_{6}=0
or
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.

By Proposition
7.24
, we are done if
a_{6}=0
. So we may assume that
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.

Now assume that
a_{6}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
It follows from (
41
) and (
43
) that
\varphi\leq 0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,2}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{5},\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,2}\left(a_{1},\ldots,a_{5},\frac{n-4k}{2},b,c,d\right)\leq\Xi(n,k).

where the last inequality follows from Proposition
7.14

(ii)
and the assumption
k\geq\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278}>\frac{n}{6}
.
This completes the proof of Proposition
7.30
.

Proposition 7.33
.

Let
(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\in\Omega_{n,k}
.
Suppose
k\in\left[\frac{n}{6},\frac{n}{4}\right]
and
a_{6}\geq\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
Then

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Proof of Proposition
7.33
.

Since

\displaystyle\Phi_{3,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\leq\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),

Proposition
7.33
is a direct consequence of Proposition
7.19
.

7.5
Proof for the main result

We present the proof of Theorem
1.3
in this section.
Recall that

\displaystyle(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)=(|\mathcal{A}_{1}|,\ldots,|\mathcal{A}_{6}|,|\mathcal{B}|,|\mathcal{C}|,|\mathcal{D}|).

Fix a sufficiently large integer
\mu>0
such that, in particular, Lemma

holds .
Let

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\quad\coloneqq\begin{cases}\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}\quad\text{or}\quad a_{6}\leq b+c,\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}\quad\text{and}\quad a_{6}\in\left[b+c,\frac{n-4k}{2}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d),&\quad\text{if}\quad e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}\quad\text{and}\quad a_{6}\geq\frac{n-4k}{2},\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)+\varphi(a_{4},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)-\left(\frac{15a_{4}^{2}}{2}+(7b+5c)a_{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\begin{cases}g_{1/2}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/2}(a_{4},b),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\leq\frac{b+c}{2},\\[5.0pt]
\hat{g}(a_{4},b)+h_{1/4}(a_{4},b),&\quad\text{if}\quad d\geq\frac{b+c}{2}.\end{cases}

|G|

\displaystyle\quad+7a_{2}^{2}+14a_{2}(a_{3}+\cdots+a_{6})+7a_{3}^{2}+15a_{3}(a_{4}+a_{5}+a_{6}).

\displaystyle\quad+(8b+5c+3d)a_{3}+4a_{3}+(7b+5c+2d)a_{3}+20a_{3}.
