Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let us consider the following three cases.

Case 1
:
k\in\left[0,\frac{n}{8}\right]
.

This case follows directly from Proposition
7.34

(i)
and (
47
).

Case 2
:
k\in\left[\frac{n}{8},\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}\right]\cup\left[\frac{n}{5},\frac{n}{4}\right]
.

By Proposition
7.34

(ii)
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
or
a_{6}\leq b+c
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{2}
and Proposition
7.13
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
and
a_{6}\in\left[b+c,\frac{3b+2c+d}{2}\right]=\left[b+c,\frac{n-4k}{2}\right]
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{2}
and Proposition
7.17
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
and
a_{6}\geq\frac{3b+2c+d}{2}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{2}
and Proposition
7.19
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{2,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Case 3
:
k\in\left[\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130},\frac{n}{5}\right]
.

By Proposition
7.34

(iii)
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\leq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
or
a_{6}\leq b+c
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{3}
and Proposition
7.29
(note that
\frac{(19982-35\sqrt{402})n}{108278}\leq\frac{(20+\sqrt{10})n}{130}
) that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{3,1}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
and
a_{6}\in\left[b+c,\frac{3b+2c+d}{2}\right]=\left[b+c,\frac{n-4k}{2}\right]
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{3}
and Proposition
7.30
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{3,2}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

Suppose that
e(\mathcal{A}_{6})\geq\frac{15a_{6}^{2}}{2}+\mu a_{6}
and
a_{6}\geq\frac{3b+2c+d}{2}=\frac{n-4k}{2}
.
Then, it follows from the definition of
\Phi_{3}
and Proposition
7.33
that

\displaystyle\Phi_{3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)

\displaystyle=\Phi_{3,3}(a_{1},\ldots,a_{6},b,c,d)\leq\Xi(n,k).

This completes the proof of Theorem
1.3
.

8
Concluding remarks

\bullet
Below, we describe the candidate set for the extremal constructions when
r\geq 5
. For convenience, we slightly abuse notation by referring to a family, such as
E_{i}(n,k,r)
, as a typical member in this class.
All constructions defined below are assumed, by default, to have exactly
n
vertices.

(i)

The vertex set of
E_{1}(n,k,r)
consists of
r
parts
X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-1}
, with sizes satisfying

\displaystyle|X|=k\quad\text{and}\quad|Y_{1}|\leq\cdots\leq|Y_{r-1}|\leq|Y_{1}|+1.

The edge set of
E_{1}(n,k,r)
is defined as

\displaystyle E_{1}(n,k)\coloneqq\binom{X}{2}\cup K[X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-1}].

(ii)

Suppose that
i\in[2,r-1]
.
The vertex set of
E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)
consists of
r+2-i
parts
X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r+1-i}
with sizes satisfying

\displaystyle|X|=ik+i-1\quad\text{and}\quad|Y_{1}|\leq\cdots\leq|Y_{r-i}|\leq|X\cup Y_{r-i+1}|\leq|Y_{1}|+1.

The edge set of
E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)
is defined as

\displaystyle E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)=\binom{X}{2}\cup K[Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-i},X\cup Y_{r-i+1}].

Note that
E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)
is defined only for
k\leq\frac{1}{i}\left(\left\lceil\frac{n}{r+1-i}\right\rceil-i+1\right)
.

The vertex set of
E_{i}^{b}(n,k,r)
consists of
r+1-i
parts
X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-i}
with sizes satisfying

\displaystyle|X|=ik+i-1\quad\text{and}\quad|Y_{1}|\leq\cdots\leq|Y_{r-i}|\leq|Y_{1}|+1.

The edge set of
E_{i}^{b}(n,k,r)
is defined as

\displaystyle E_{i}^{b}(n,k,r)=\binom{X}{2}\cup K[X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-i}].

(iii)

The vertex set of
E_{r}(n,k,r)
consists of
2r-1
parts
X,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-1},Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r-1}
with sizes satisfying

\displaystyle|X|=rk+r-1-(r-2)(n-rk-r+1)-j,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}|Y_{i}|=(r-2)(n-rk-r+1)+j,\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle|Y_{1}\cup Z_{1}|\leq\cdots\leq|Y_{r-1}\cup Z_{r-1}|\leq|Y_{1}\cup Z_{1}|+1,

where
j\in[0,r-1]
is an arbitrary integer.
The edge set of
E_{r}(n,k,r)
is defined as

\displaystyle E_{r}(n,k,r)=\binom{X}{2}\cup K[X,Y_{1}\cup\cdots\cup Y_{r-1}]\cup K[Y_{1}\cup Z_{1},\ldots,Y_{r-1}\cup Z_{r-1}].

Note that
E_{r}(n,k,r)
is defined only for
k\geq\frac{(r-2)n}{r(r-1)}+\frac{1-r}{r}
.

It is easy to verify that all the graphs defined above have a
K_{r}
-matching number of
k
. We conjecture that
\mathrm{ex}(n,(k+1)K_{r})
is asymptotically attained by these constructions.

Conjecture 8.1
.

Let
r\geq 5
and
n\geq rk\geq 0
be integers.
Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{ex}(n,(k+1)K_{r})

\displaystyle=\max\big\{|E_{1}(n,k,r)|,~|E_{2}^{a}(n,k,r)|,~|E_{2}^{b}(n,k,r)|,\cdots,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad|E_{r-1}^{a}(n,k,r)|,~|E_{r-1}^{b}(n,k,r)|,~|E_{r}(n,k,r)|\big\}+O(n).

Remark
.
Elementary calculations show that
|E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)|\leq|E_{2}^{a}(n,k,r)|
holds for all
i\in[3,r-1]
and for all
(n,k,r)
for which
E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)
is defined. Consequently, there are only
r+1
classes of extremal constructions in the conjecture above. Nevertheless, we include
E_{i}^{a}(n,k,r)
in case they may assist in the proof.

\bullet
Recently Lo–Williams
[
LW24
]
considered the following extension of the Corrádi–Hajnal Theorem in edge-colored graphs:
Given an edge-colored graph
G
on
n
vertices, let
\delta^{c}(G)
denote the largest integer
m
such that, for every vertex
v\in V(G)
, there are at least
m
distinct colors on edges incident to
v
.
Lo–Williams
[
LW24
]
obtained several bounds on
\delta^{c}(G)
to ensure that
G
contains a perfect rainbow-
K_{r}
-tiling (we refer the reader to
[
LW24
]
for the definition). In particular, they proved that for
r\geq 5
,

\displaystyle\delta^{c}(G)\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{(2r-3)r}+o(1)\right)n

ensures the existence of a perfect rainbow-
K_{r}
-tiling in
G
.
Unfortunately, this bound is somewhat distant from their conjectured bound
\left(1-\frac{r-1}{r^{2}-2}\right)n
, and they remarked:

For
r\geq 5
, we are unable to obtain any “reasonable” upper bound for the existence of a rainbow-
K_{r}
-tiling. One reason is that we are unaware of a density version of the Hajnal–Szemerédi theorem, that is, the Turán number for
t
vertex-disjoint
K_{r-1}
.

Using the density version of the Corrádi–Hajnal Theorem (i.e. Theorem
1.2
), Lo–Williams improved
3
3

3

Their proof seems to give the better bound
\delta^{c}(G)\geq\left(\frac{3+\sqrt{6}}{6}+o(1)\right)n\approx(0.908248+o(1))n
.

\delta^{c}(G)
to
\left(\frac{4+\sqrt{26}}{10}+o(1)\right)n\approx(0.909902+o(1))n
for
r=4
.
Similarly, using Theorem
1.3
, we can improve the bound
\delta^{c}(G)\geq\left(\frac{34}{35}+o(1)\right)n\approx(0.971428+o(1))n
of Lo–Williams to
\delta^{c}(G)\geq\left(\frac{8+\sqrt{37}}{15}+o(1)\right)n\approx\left(0.93885+o(1)\right)n
for
r=5
.

Appendix A
Calculations

For every real number
\gamma\geq 0
and integer
d\geq 0
, let

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{d}(\gamma)\coloneqq\left\{(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\colon\sum_{i\in[d+1]}x_{i}\leq\gamma~\mathrm{and}~x_{i}\geq 0~\mathrm{for}~i\in[d+1]\right\}.

For real numbers
b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10}
, let

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})\coloneqq

\displaystyle-\frac{3x_{2}^{2}}{112}-\frac{3x_{3}^{2}}{56}-\frac{3x_{4}^{2}}{32}-\frac{x_{5}^{2}}{8}-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{6}+\cdots+x_{10}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{4}\left((x_{2}+\cdots+x_{5})x_{10}+(x_{3}+x_{4}+x_{5})x_{9}+(x_{4}+x_{5})x_{8}+x_{5}x_{7}\right)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=2}^{10}\frac{(i-1)bx_{i}}{10}.

We prove the following upper bound for
\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})
. All Mathematica calculations for this proposition are available at
https://xliu2022.github.io/DensityHSK4.nb
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\gamma,b\geq 0
be real numbers.
We have

\displaystyle\max_{(x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})\in\mathbb{S}^{8}(\gamma)}\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{9b\gamma}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{27b^{2}}{50},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{6b}{5},\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{43b\gamma}{150}+\frac{103b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15},\frac{56b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{232}+\frac{57b\gamma}{290}+\frac{113b^{2}}{435},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{56b}{15},\frac{38b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{151b^{2}}{150},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{38b}{5}.\end{cases}

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Fix
\gamma,b\geq 0
.
Choose
(x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})\in\mathbb{S}^{8}(\gamma)
such that

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})=\eta_{\ast}\coloneqq\max_{(z_{2},\ldots,z_{10})\in\mathbb{S}^{8}(\gamma)}\eta(b,z_{2},\ldots,z_{10}).

Claim A.2
.

We may assume that
x_{2}+\cdots+x_{9}=0
or
x_{10}=0
.

Proof of Claim
A.2
.

Suppose that
x_{2}+\cdots+x_{9}\neq 0
and
x_{10}\neq 0
.
Let

\displaystyle q_{1}(y)\coloneqq\eta\left(b,(1-y)x_{2},\ldots,(1-y)x_{9},x_{10}+(x_{2}+\cdots+x_{9})y\right).

Notice that
q_{1}(y)
is a quadratic polynomial in
y
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
y^{2}
in
q_{1}(y)
is

\displaystyle\frac{39x_{2}^{2}}{112}+\frac{9x_{3}^{2}}{28}+\frac{9x_{4}^{2}}{32}+\frac{x_{5}^{2}}{4}

\displaystyle+\frac{3}{4}\left(x_{2}(x_{3}+\cdots+x_{9})+x_{3}(x_{4}+\cdots+x_{8})+x_{4}(x_{5}+x_{6}+x_{7})+x_{5}x_{6}\right)\geq 0.

So it follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})=q_{1}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{1}\left(-\frac{x_{10}}{x_{2}+\cdots+x_{9}}\right),q_{1}(1)\right\},

which means that we may assume that
x_{2}+\cdots+x_{9}=0
or
x_{10}=0
.

Claim A.3
.

Suppose that
x_{10}=0
. Then we may assume that
x_{3}+\cdots+x_{8}=0
or
x_{9}=0
.

Proof of Claim
A.3
.

Suppose that
x_{3}+\cdots+x_{8}\neq 0
and
x_{9}\neq 0
.
Similar to the proof of Claim
A.2
, let

\displaystyle q_{2}(y)\coloneqq\eta\left(b,x_{2},(1-y)x_{3},\ldots,(1-y)x_{8},x_{9}+(x_{3}+\cdots+x_{8})y,0\right).

Notice that
q_{2}(y)
is a quadratic polynomial in
y
, and straightforward calculations show that the coefficient of
y^{2}
in
q_{2}(y)
is

\displaystyle\frac{9x_{3}^{2}}{28}+\frac{9x_{4}^{2}}{32}+\frac{x_{5}^{2}}{4}+\frac{3}{4}\left(x_{3}(x_{4}+\cdots+x_{8})+x_{4}(x_{5}+x_{6}+x_{7})+x_{5}x_{6}\right)\geq 0.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},\ldots,x_{10})=q_{2}(0)\leq\max\left\{q_{2}\left(-\frac{x_{9}}{x_{3}+\cdots+x_{8}}\right),q_{2}(1)\right\},

meaning that we can assume that
x_{3}+\cdots+x_{8}=0
or
x_{9}=0
.

Similar proofs to those in Claims
A.2
and
A.3
yield the following results.

Claim A.4
.

Suppose that
x_{9}=x_{10}=0
. Then we may assume that
x_{4}+x_{5}+x_{6}+x_{7}=0
or
x_{8}=0
.

Claim A.5
.

Suppose that
x_{8}=x_{9}=x_{10}=0
. Then we may assume that
x_{5}+x_{6}=0
or
x_{7}=0
.

It follows from Claims
A.2
,
A.3
,
A.4
, and
A.5
that

\displaystyle\eta_{\ast}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\eta(b,0,\ldots,0,x_{10}),~\eta(b,x_{2},0,\ldots,0,x_{9},0),~\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},0,\ldots,0,x_{8},0,0),\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},0,0,x_{7},0,0,0),~\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},0,\ldots,0)\right\}.

(55)

Next, we consider respectively the upper bounds for
\eta(b,0,\ldots,0,x_{10})
,
\eta(b,x_{2},0,\ldots,0,x_{9},0)
,
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},0,\ldots,0,x_{8},0,0)
,
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},0,0,x_{7},0,0,0)
, and
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},0,\ldots,0)
.

For
\eta(b,0,\ldots,0,x_{10})
, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,0,\ldots,0,x_{10})=-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{10}-\frac{6b}{5}\right)^{2}+\frac{27b^{2}}{50}\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{9b\gamma}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{27b^{2}}{50},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma>\frac{6b}{5}.\end{cases}

(56)

For
\eta(b,x_{2},0,\ldots,0,x_{9},0)
, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},0,\ldots,0,x_{9},0)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{112}\left(x_{2}-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{9}-\frac{16b}{15}\right)^{2}+\frac{13b^{2}}{25}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}+\frac{343b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{14b}{15},\frac{44b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{13b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{44b}{15}.\end{cases}

(57)

Here, the last inequality is derived using Mathematica but can also be proven without a computer, as demonstrated in Proposition
A.6
.

For
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},0,\ldots,0,x_{8},0,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},0,\ldots,0,x_{8},0,0)

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{3}{112}\left(x_{2}-\frac{28}{15}b\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{56}\left(x_{3}-\frac{28}{15}b\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{8}-\frac{14}{15}b\right)^{2}+\frac{91b^{2}}{150}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{7b\gamma}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{64}+\frac{21b\gamma}{80}+\frac{7b^{2}}{48},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{2b}{3},\frac{26b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{176}+\frac{7b\gamma}{44}+\frac{259b^{2}}{1100},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{26b}{15},\frac{14b}{3}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{91b^{2}}{150},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{14b}{3}.\end{cases}

(58)

Similarly, the inequality above is derived using Mathematica; but can also be proven using a similar argument as in Proposition
A.6
. We omit the tedious calculations here.

For
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},0,0,x_{7},0,0,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},0,0,x_{7},0,0,0)

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{3}{112}\left(x_{2}-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{56}\left(x_{3}-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{32}\left(x_{4}-\frac{8b}{5}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{7}-\frac{4b}{5}\right)^{2}+\frac{19b^{2}}{25}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{3b\gamma}{5},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{9b\gamma}{25}+\frac{6b^{2}}{125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{2b}{5},\frac{16b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{32}+\frac{4b\gamma}{15}+\frac{22b^{2}}{225},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{16b}{15},\frac{8b}{3}b\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{208}+\frac{23b\gamma}{130}+\frac{212b^{2}}{975},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{8b}{3},\frac{92b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{19b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{92b}{15}.\end{cases}

(59)

Similarly, the inequality above is derived using Mathematica; but can also be proven using a similar argument as in Proposition
A.6
. We omit the tedious calculations here.

For
\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},0,\ldots,0)
, we have

\displaystyle\eta(b,x_{2},x_{3},x_{4},x_{5},x_{6},0,\ldots,0)

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{112}\left(x_{2}-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{56}\left(x_{3}-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{3}{32}\left(x_{4}-\frac{8b}{5}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{8}\left(x_{5}-\frac{8b}{5}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{8}\left(x_{6}-\frac{2b}{3}\right)^{2}+\frac{151b^{2}}{150}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{b\gamma}{2},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{32}+\frac{17b\gamma}{40}+\frac{b^{2}}{200},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{2b}{15},\frac{2b}{3}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{64}+\frac{29b\gamma}{80}+\frac{31b^{2}}{1200},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{2b}{3},\frac{26b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{43b\gamma}{150}+\frac{103b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{26b}{15},\frac{56b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{232}+\frac{57b\gamma}{290}+\frac{113b^{2}}{435},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{56b}{15},\frac{38b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{151b^{2}}{150},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{38b}{5}.\end{cases}

(60)

Similarly, the inequality above is derived using Mathematica; but can also be proven using a similar argument as in Proposition
A.6
. We omit the tedious calculations here.

Merging (this is done using Mathematica) the piecewise functions given by (
56
), (
57
), (
58
), (
59
), and (
60
), and combining them with (
55
), we obtain

\displaystyle\eta_{\ast}\leq\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{9b\gamma}{10},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{6b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{27b^{2}}{50},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{6b}{5},\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{43b\gamma}{150}+\frac{103b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{(86-4\sqrt{210})b}{15},\frac{56b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{232}+\frac{57b\gamma}{290}+\frac{113b^{2}}{435},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{56b}{15},\frac{38b}{5}\right],\\[5.0pt]
\frac{151b^{2}}{150},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{38b}{5}.\end{cases}

This completes the proof of Proposition
A.1
.

We present the calculations for (
57
) in the proposition below.

Proposition A.6
.

Let
\gamma,b\geq 0
be real numbers and
\Phi(x,y)\coloneqq-\frac{3}{112}\left(x-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{3}{8}\left(y-\frac{16b}{15}\right)^{2}
.
We have

\displaystyle\max_{(x,y)\in\mathbb{S}^{1}(\gamma)}\Phi(x,y)=\begin{cases}-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{14b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{14b}{15},\frac{44b}{15}\right],\\[5.0pt]
0,&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{44b}{15}.\end{cases}

Proof of Proposition
A.6
.

Fix
\gamma,b\geq 0
.
Fix
(x,y)\in\mathbb{S}^{1}(\gamma)
such that
\Phi(x,y)
is maximized over
\mathbb{S}^{1}(\gamma)
.
The case
\gamma\geq\frac{44b}{15}=\frac{28b}{15}+\frac{16b}{15}
is clear, so we may assume that
0\leq\gamma<\frac{44b}{15}
.

Case 1
:
x=0
.

We now have a one-variable optimization problem:

maximize

\displaystyle\Phi\left(0,y\right)=-\frac{7b^{2}}{75}-\frac{3}{8}\left(y-\frac{16b}{15}\right)^{2},

subject to

\displaystyle y\in[0,\gamma].

It follows from Fact
7.6
that

\displaystyle\max_{y\in[0,\gamma]}\Phi\left(0,y\right)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\frac{7b^{2}}{75},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{16b}{15},\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma<\frac{16b}{15}.\end{cases}

(61)

Case 2
:
y=0
.

Note that

\displaystyle\Phi(x,0)=-\frac{3}{112}\left(x-\frac{28b}{15}\right)^{2}-\frac{32b^{2}}{75}.

Similar to Case 1, we have

\displaystyle\max_{x\in[0,\gamma]}\Phi(x,0)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\frac{32b^{2}}{75},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{18b}{15},\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{112}+\frac{b\gamma}{10}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma<\frac{18b}{15}.\end{cases}

(62)

Case 3
:
x+y=\gamma
.

Note that

\displaystyle\Phi(x,\gamma-x)=-\frac{45}{112}\left(x-\frac{14(15\gamma-14b)}{225}\right)^{2}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}.

Since
\gamma\geq\frac{114(15\gamma-14b)}{225}
for all
\gamma\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\max_{x\in[0,\gamma]}\Phi(x,\gamma-x)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma\geq\frac{14b}{15},\\[5.0pt]
-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25},&\quad\text{if}\quad\gamma<\frac{14b}{15}.\end{cases}

(63)

Case 4
:
x>0
,
y>0
, and
x+y<\gamma
.

It follows from the maximality of
\Phi(x,y)
that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Phi(x,y)}{\partial x}=-\frac{6}{112}\left(x-\frac{28b}{15}\right)=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\partial\Phi(x,y)}{\partial y}=-\frac{3}{4}\left(y-\frac{16b}{15}\right)=0.

This implies that
(x,y)=\left(\frac{28b}{15},\frac{16b}{15}\right)
and thus
\gamma>\frac{28b}{15}+\frac{16b}{15}=\frac{44b}{15}
, contradicting the assumption that
\gamma<\frac{44b}{15}
.

Next, we compare the lower bounds (
61
), (
62
), and (
63
) obtained above.

Since

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25}\right)-\left(-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{112}+\frac{b\gamma}{10}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25}\right)=\frac{\gamma(392b-195\gamma)}{560}\geq 0

for
\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{14b}{15}\right]
,
we have

\displaystyle\max_{(x,y)\in\mathbb{S}^{1}(\gamma)}\Phi(x,y)=-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25}\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma\in\left[0,\frac{14b}{15}\right].

Since

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}\right)-\left(-\frac{7b^{2}}{75}\right)=\frac{238b^{2}}{1125}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}\geq 0,\quad\text{for}\quad k\in\left[\frac{16b}{15},\frac{44b}{15}\right],

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}\right)-\left(-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{8}+\frac{4b\gamma}{5}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25}\right)=\frac{7(14b-15\gamma)^{2}}{4500}\geq 0,

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}\right)-\left(-\frac{32b^{2}}{75}\right)=\frac{238b^{2}}{1125}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}\geq 0,\quad\text{for}\quad k\in\left[\frac{18b}{15},\frac{44b}{15}\right],

and

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}\right)-\left(-\frac{3\gamma^{2}}{112}+\frac{b\gamma}{10}-\frac{13b^{2}}{25}\right)=\frac{(196b+15\gamma)^{2}}{126000}\geq 0,

we have

\displaystyle\max_{(x,y)\in\mathbb{S}^{1}(\gamma)}\Phi(x,y)=-\frac{\gamma^{2}}{40}+\frac{11b\gamma}{75}-\frac{242b^{2}}{1125}\quad\text{for}\quad\gamma\in\left[\frac{14b}{15},\frac{44b}{15}\right].

This completes the proof of Proposition
A.6
.

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
6.6

Proof of Proposition
6.6
.

Let
S\subseteq V(H)
be a set of size
s\geq h-12\alpha-42
.
Let
S_{i}\coloneqq S\setminus(Y_{i}\cup Z_{i})
and
x_{i}\coloneqq|S_{i}|
for
i\in[3]
, noting that
x_{i}\leq\alpha+1
for every
i\in[3]
. Let
x\coloneqq x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3}
.

Since
G-X
is
3
-partite, simple calculations show that

\displaystyle|G[S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cup S_{3}]|

\displaystyle=x_{1}x_{2}+x_{2}x_{3}+x_{3}x_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}0,&\quad\text{if}\quad x\leq\alpha+1,\\[5.0pt]
(\alpha+1)(x-\alpha-1),&\quad\text{if}\quad\alpha+1\leq x\leq 2(\alpha+1),\\[5.0pt]
(\alpha+1)^{2}+(2\alpha+2)(x-2\alpha-2),&\quad\text{if}\quad x\geq 2(\alpha+1).\end{cases}

Case 1
:
x\leq\alpha

We have

\displaystyle|G[S]|

\displaystyle=|G[S\cap X]|+|G[S\cap X,S\cap(Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}\cup Y_{3})]|+|G[S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cup S_{3}]|

\displaystyle\geq\binom{s-x}{2}\geq\binom{s-\alpha}{2}.

Case 2
:
\alpha+1\leq x\leq 2(\alpha+1)
.

We have

\displaystyle|G[S]|

\displaystyle=|G[S\cap X]|+|G[S\cap X,S\cap(Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}\cup Y_{3})]|+|G[S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cup S_{3}]|

\displaystyle\geq\binom{s-x}{2}+(s-x)(x-\alpha)+(\alpha+1)(x-\alpha-1)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(x-2\alpha-\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\alpha^{2}-\frac{s}{2}+\alpha+\frac{1}{8}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{2}\left(\alpha+1-2\alpha-\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\alpha^{2}-\frac{s}{2}+\alpha+\frac{1}{8}

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2}-\frac{s-\alpha}{2}=\binom{s-\alpha}{2}.

Case 3
:
x\geq 2(\alpha+1)
.

We have

\displaystyle|G[S]|

\displaystyle=|G[S\cap X]|+|G[S\cap X,S\cap(Y_{1}\cup Y_{2}\cup Y_{3})]|+|G[S_{1}\cup S_{2}\cup S_{3}]|

\displaystyle\geq\binom{s-x}{2}+(s-x)(x-\alpha)+(\alpha+1)^{2}+(2\alpha+2)(x-2\alpha-2)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(x-3\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\frac{3\alpha^{2}}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+\frac{3\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{8}

\displaystyle\geq-\frac{1}{2}\left(2\alpha+2-3\alpha-\frac{5}{2}\right)^{2}+\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\frac{3\alpha^{2}}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+\frac{3\alpha}{2}+\frac{1}{8}

\displaystyle=\frac{s^{2}}{2}-\alpha s+\alpha^{2}-\frac{s}{2}+\alpha=\binom{s-\alpha}{2}+\frac{\alpha^{2}+\alpha}{2}\geq\binom{s-\alpha}{2}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
6.6
.
